Every change to the Ankra platform, newest first. Use the tag filter beside this page to narrow it to `Pipelines`, `Security`, `Cost`, `Clusters`, `AI`, `GitOps`, `CLI`, `API` or `Fixes` - the selection is in the URL, so a filtered view is a link you can share. Subscribe at [docs.ankra.ai/changelog/rss.xml](https://docs.ankra.ai/changelog/rss.xml) to get each change as it ships.

Changes up to the end of August 2026 are kept as monthly records. From September 2026 each change has its own entry, and each week's smaller fixes are collected in one **Also shipped** entry. Command-line releases have their own [CLI changelog](/integrations/ankra-cli-changelog).

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: starting a new playground" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: starting a new playground" }}>
**Starting a new playground** - while the replacement for a playground that did not start is being ordered, **Go straight to my cluster** now waits for the new one. It opened the one that failed. [→](/get-started/playground#start-a-playground-and-put-a-website-on-it)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "What changed on a cluster, listed beside its charts" }}>
## What changed on a cluster, listed beside its charts

**What changed** on a cluster's **Metrics** lists the window's events by day in a panel: when, what happened, who started it when Ankra knows, and how it ended. An operation's name opens that operation. The panel stays open while the page refreshes, and Ankra's own checks are behind a switch.

[What changed →](/platform/cluster-metrics#what-changed)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: merge the setup before its checks pass" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: merge the setup before its checks pass" }}>
**Merge the setup before its checks pass** - on Go live, **Merge when the checks pass** now waits for the setup pull request's checks and merges it as soon as they do, with the run's progress on the page. Pressing Merge while Ankra was still building used to answer "Ankra is still building commit ..." and merge nothing. After the merge, the page waits for the first build instead of offering to start a second one. [→](/guides/deliver-your-app#ship-from-the-portal)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: an UpCloud account limit no longer stops a node for good" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: an UpCloud account limit no longer stops a node for good" }}>
**An UpCloud account limit no longer stops a node for good** - when UpCloud refuses a server because of an account limit, a trial or missing credit, the failed step now gives UpCloud's reason and Ankra keeps retrying. Once the account allows the server, Ankra creates it. It used to treat a limit as a refused API token and give up until you reconciled the cluster. [→](/guides/upcloud-clusters#troubleshooting)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: your first pipeline runs on your playground" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: your first pipeline runs on your playground" }}>
**Your first pipeline runs on your playground** - an organisation whose only cluster is its free playground now runs its pipelines there, with nothing to set. Its first setup pull request used to get a check titled "Choose a CI cluster for this organisation" and no run. A cluster you confirmed as non-production still comes first. [→](/pipelines/where-pipelines-run#your-own-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: tools a conversation was not offered" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: tools a conversation was not offered" }}>
**Tools a conversation was not offered** - in the Ask panel, Ankra AI no longer raises a card, or runs anything, for a tool your conversation was not offered, such as creating a cluster on a provider your organisation does not have. It says the tool is not available instead. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Merge the setup pull request from Go live, without GitHub" }}>
## Merge the setup pull request from Go live, without GitHub

At **Prepare the setup**, **Merge and continue** takes Ankra's setup pull request out of draft and merges it for you, so you no longer need to know how to do that on GitHub. Ankra merges only the commit it built, once that build passed. When something stops the merge, such as a conflict, the repository's merge train or branch protection, the page says what and nothing is merged.

[Ship your app →](/guides/deliver-your-app#ship-from-the-portal)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ankra AI in Slack and Teams after loading tools" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ankra AI in Slack and Teams after loading tools" }}>
**Ankra AI in Slack and Teams after loading tools** - a turn that loads more tools no longer stops early on the newest Claude models, and a request the AI service rejects ends with the provider's reason, on your own AI key too. It used to say the connection to the AI service failed, or, on your own key, to try again. The Ask panel has worked this way since 18 November. [→](/platform/ai-provider#when-the-ai-service-rejects-a-request)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: what Ask Ankra cannot do here" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: what Ask Ankra cannot do here" }}>
**What Ask Ankra cannot do here** - when a conversation has no tool for what you ask, such as listing a cluster's variables, Ask Ankra now says so and, where one exists, gives you the `ankra` command or the Ankra page that does it. It used to say it had no tool for that and ask you for details it could not use. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: where an image is declared, when Ankra cannot be sure" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: where an image is declared, when Ankra cannot be sure" }}>
**Where an image is declared, when Ankra cannot be sure** - Ask Ankra no longer calls a running workload declared by no stack when its cluster takes chart values from a place Ankra does not read: a k3s HelmChart's `valuesSecrets`, Fleet's `valuesFrom`, or an Argo CD values file in another source. It also no longer gives a Git line for a stack member when another member writes the same file. [→](/guides/fix-a-vulnerable-image#when-no-application-of-yours-builds-the-image)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a publish waits for its signed statement" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a publish waits for its signed statement" }}>
**A publish waits for its signed statement** - for an application on `enforce`, a publish step whose registry times out while storing the image's signed statement now retries for up to five minutes, and then fails. Re-running the run signs the image it published. It used to pass without the statement, and the deploy that followed refused the image. [→](/security/deploy-provenance#when-a-deploy-is-refused)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: back from GitHub, the repository list fills in by itself" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: back from GitHub, the repository list fills in by itself" }}>
**Back from GitHub, the repository list fills in by itself** - after you install the Ankra app on GitHub, **New application** and **Put my app online** no longer ask you to connect GitHub again while Ankra sets the new connection up. They say **Getting your GitHub connection ready.**, then choose the connection and list its repositories within seconds. [→](/guides/deliver-your-app#ship-from-the-portal)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Add a cluster from the Infrastructure overview" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Add a cluster from the Infrastructure overview" }}>
**Add a cluster from the Infrastructure overview** - the main button on **Infrastructure** is now **Add a cluster**, which opens the page where you choose how to get a cluster into Ankra. It used to be **Manage backups**; backups are still under **Backups and storage** on the same page. A role without permission to create clusters sees no button there. [→](/guides/import-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: no Kubernetes version before one is reported" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: no Kubernetes version before one is reported" }}>
**No Kubernetes version before one is reported** - a cluster that is still being created or connecting, such as a new playground, no longer shows **Kubernetes 0.0.0** on its Overview or when you connect your own cluster. The Overview says **Kubernetes version not reported** until one is known. [→](/platform/cluster-overview-and-health#1-open-the-cluster’s-overview)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-24" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: fix candidates for images on your Ankra registry" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: fix candidates for images on your Ankra registry" }}>
**Fix candidates for images on your Ankra registry** - when Ask Ankra looks for the tag that fixes a vulnerability, it now reads those images with your organisation's pull credential, instead of saying the registry could not be read. [→](/guides/fix-a-vulnerable-image#when-no-application-of-yours-builds-the-image)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a provenance refusal that names the run" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a provenance refusal that names the run" }}>
**A provenance refusal that names the run** - when an application on `enforce` refuses an image, the message now names it by repository and tag. It says which pipeline run published it, why that run did not sign it, and the fix for that reason, such as re-running that run. It used to tell you to publish through the pipeline even when the pipeline had. [→](/security/deploy-provenance#when-a-deploy-is-refused)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: when Ankra AI cannot read your stacks" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: when Ankra AI cannot read your stacks" }}>
**When Ankra AI cannot read your stacks** - while installing an add-on or showing an add-on's dependencies, Ankra AI now says when it could not read the cluster's stacks, and why. It used to answer as though the cluster had no stacks. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ankra Support stays signed in" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ankra Support stays signed in" }}>
**Ankra Support stays signed in** - a session on [Ankra Support](https://support.ankra.app) now lasts up to eight hours instead of five minutes, so the page no longer reloads through Platform, or lands on Platform's support page, every few minutes while you work. [→](/platform/support#ankra-support-the-ai-chat)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: charts on an application's Overview read one place" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: charts on an application's Overview read one place" }}>
**Charts on an application's Overview read one place** - a chart on an application's Overview, such as **Requests by status**, no longer says "The application runs in 2 places" when the newest deploy failed while the version before it still serves. When the environment runs on several clusters, the chart reads the one **Health** reads and names it under its title. [→](/platform/application-page#a-chart-as-a-card)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra hosted logs: 5 GB a month free, then EUR 0.25 per GB, from 1 January 2027" }}>
## Ankra hosted logs: 5 GB a month free, then EUR 0.25 per GB, from 1 January 2027

Ankra hosted logs stay included until 31 December 2026. From 1 January 2027 each organisation gets 5 GB of log text a month free, across all its clusters, and each GB above that costs EUR 0.25, with lines kept for 7 days. At the free 5 GB, sending pauses until the next month unless an owner turns on paid logs with a monthly limit, so nothing is charged without that yes. Ankra built-in metrics stay included in every plan.

[Ankra hosted logs →](/guides/hosted-logs#price)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Security"]} description="Also shipped this week: daily security report emails" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: daily security report emails" }}>
**Daily security report emails** - a cluster's scheduled security report can now go out every day, as well as weekly or monthly, and its trends compare with the day before. Set it with `ankra security report-schedule set --frequency daily` or with the MCP tool `set_cluster_security_report_schedule`. [→](/guides/security-reports)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: fix candidates from a slow registry" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: fix candidates from a slow registry" }}>
**Fix candidates from a slow registry** - when an image's registry is slow, Ask Ankra still answers which newer tag fixes a vulnerability, marking the tags it could not read as not verified, instead of running out of time. A fixed version now counts only on the distribution release it was published for, so an image moved to a newer Alpine release is no longer read as fixed by the older release's fix. [→](/guides/fix-a-vulnerable-image#when-no-application-of-yours-builds-the-image)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a run waits for a cluster that is still starting" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a run waits for a cluster that is still starting" }}>
**A run waits for a cluster that is still starting** - when you create a development cluster and add an application before the cluster is online, the setup pull request's run now waits for the cluster's agent and says so: "Ankra is still starting cluster ...". It used to end with a message telling you to connect the agent, and you had to run it again. A cluster you import is not waited for. [→](/pipelines/where-pipelines-run#your-own-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: pipelines on an IPv6-only cluster reach IPv4-only hosts" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: pipelines on an IPv6-only cluster reach IPv4-only hosts" }}>
**Pipelines on an IPv6-only cluster reach IPv4-only hosts** - on an Ankra Cloud cluster without IPv4, a pipeline's scans and report uploads failed with "connection refused" when the host had only an IPv4 address, such as the Ankra registry. Steps on `egress-https`, and the pods Ankra runs beside them, now reach such hosts through the cluster's NAT64 gateway. Private and metadata addresses stay out of reach. [→](/guides/pipeline-reference#fields-common-to-every-stage)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a sign-in that did not complete" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a sign-in that did not complete" }}>
**A sign-in that did not complete** - now says why on Ankra's sign-in page, with **Try again** and **Use another account**. The single sign-on address `platform.ankra.app/auth/sso` opens the sign-in page, where your work email address leads to your organisation's provider. On **Verify your email address**, **Use a different address** signs you out and opens **Create your Ankra account**. [→](/platform/account-security#the-sign-in-page)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: the password steps match the sign-in page" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: the password steps match the sign-in page" }}>
**The password steps match the sign-in page** - entering a password, choosing one when you sign up and resetting one now look like the rest of Ankra's sign-in page, and the password rules tick off as you type. [→](/platform/account-security#passwords-and-resets)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Microsoft on Create your Ankra account" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Microsoft on Create your Ankra account" }}>
**Microsoft on Create your Ankra account** - choosing **Microsoft** when you create an account now takes you to Microsoft and back signed in, instead of ending on a page that says the sign-in did not complete. [→](/platform/account-security#the-sign-in-page)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters"]} description="Also shipped this week: add-ons on a cluster's Overview open their page" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: add-ons on a cluster's Overview open their page" }}>
**Add-ons on a cluster's Overview** - each add-on in the Overview's **Add-ons** card now opens that add-on's page, with its health, workloads and configuration. Until now you went through **Browse add-ons** and found it again. [→](/platform/cluster-overview-and-health#2-read-the-overview-from-the-top)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters"]} description="Also shipped this week: help when a playground takes too long" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: help when a playground takes too long" }}>
**Help when a playground takes too long** - a playground still setting up five minutes after it started now says **This is taking longer than it should**, on its cluster's Overview and on the playground page. One press on **Yes, open a support ticket** sends Ankra support a ticket with the cluster and the setup step it is on. [→](/guides/cluster-sandbox#if-it-takes-longer-than-five-minutes)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a yes in plan mode, Slack and Teams" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a yes in plan mode, Slack and Teams" }}>
**A yes in plan mode, Slack and Teams** - a short reply such as "yes" to a change Ankra AI proposed now keeps the tools of that work in plan mode, and in Ankra AI in Slack and Microsoft Teams. There it used to get no cluster tools, so it could not do what you had agreed to. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Security", "Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Ignore an add-on's advisories until a chart release fixes them" }}>
## Ignore an add-on's advisories until a chart release fixes them

When no chart release fixes an add-on's security advisories yet, someone with the `security.triage` permission can choose **Ignore for now** on the cluster overview's Security card or on the add-on's page. The ignore covers only the advisories the add-on has then, never one published later, and ends by itself once a chart release fixes them. **Stop ignoring** brings the add-on back at once.

[Security advisories no chart release fixes yet →](/concepts/addons#security-advisories-no-chart-release-fixes-yet)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: delete previews" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: delete previews" }}>
**Delete previews** - `ankra cluster delete --dry-run`, and the delete previews of Ask Ankra and the MCP tools `delete_resource` and `delete_pod`, leave the object and its dependents in place from agent 2.1.1354. On agent 2.1.1346 to 2.1.1352, a preview deletes them. [→](/reference/cli/cluster#ankra-cluster-delete)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: the agent stays off small control planes" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: the agent stays off small control planes" }}>
**The agent stays off small control planes** - from agent 2.1.1354 the Ankra agent prefers worker nodes, so it no longer lands on a small control-plane node while workers have room, where Kubernetes could evict it when the node ran low on memory and the cluster read as offline. When no worker can take it, it runs where it did before. [→](/reference/agent-helm-values#where-the-agent-runs)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: CI playgrounds move only runs they can help" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: CI playgrounds move only runs they can help" }}>
**CI playgrounds move only runs they can help** - a run is no longer moved to a CI playground when one of its stages selects a node pool, an architecture or a runtime class the playground does not have. A cluster now counts as too small only after a stage of the same repository ran out of room on it and has not succeeded there since, not after any run of the organisation did. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#when-a-run-does-not-fit-the-ci-playground)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters"]} description="Also shipped this week: Add a cluster asks for the payment method on its first step" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Add a cluster asks for the payment method on its first step" }}>
**Add a cluster asks for the payment method on its first step** - an organisation without a payment method now reads "A payment method is required to create this cluster" on the first step, with a link to add one. It used to learn it on the review step, after filling the form in. The row goes by itself once the payment method is added, and what you typed stays. [→](/guides/create-a-cluster#before-you-start)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Go live names a run that needs a payment method" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Go live names a run that needs a payment method" }}>
**Go live names a run that needs a payment method** - when a setup pull request's checks or a first build did not start because the organisation has no payment method, the application's Go live page now says so, with **Open billing**. It used to say it could not read the checks, or to ask for a first build that would be refused again. [→](/guides/why-was-i-refused#the-free-allowance-without-a-payment-method)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters"]} description="Also shipped this week: a new cluster offers Add an application" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a new cluster offers Add an application" }}>
**A new cluster offers Add an application** - when a cluster you created is ready, its page now has **Add an application** beside **Open the cluster**, so the next step is one click. [→](/guides/create-a-cluster#create-the-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a chart version with or without a leading v" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a chart version with or without a leading v" }}>
**A chart version with or without a leading v** - an add-on pinned to `1.20.4` no longer reads "1.20.4 is not in the cert-manager index" when the registry lists the same release as `v1.20.4`. The version field treats the two as one release, as Helm does, and lists it once. [→](/concepts/addons#what-an-add-on-is-made-of)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: builds during a platform hiccup" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: builds during a platform hiccup" }}>
**Builds during a platform hiccup** - a build on Ankra platform builders no longer fails as "No builder capacity is configured for this platform yet" when Ankra briefly cannot read whether its builders are running. It waits for a builder instead, and a build that finds none running now says exactly that. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#ankra-platform-builders)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["AI", "Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "In the web chat, Ask Ankra can now show the merge train, take a pull request off it, and run, rerun or cancel a pipeline" }}>
## In the web chat, Ask Ankra can now show the merge train, take a pull request off it, and run, rerun or cancel a pipeline

Ask Ankra in the web app and `ankra chat` now reads a repository's merge train, pipeline runs, schedules, reports and caches. In Agent mode it can also take a pull request off the train, start, rerun or cancel a run, clear a cache, and manage schedules and connected repositories. Each change asks first with a card and runs as you when you confirm. It used to answer that it had no tool for the merge train.

[Pipelines and the merge train →](/platform/ai-assistant#pipelines-and-the-merge-train)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: dependency fixes in a new pull request" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: dependency fixes in a new pull request" }}>
**Dependency fixes in a new pull request** - Ask Ankra can fix an application's dependency findings in a new pull request that carries the lockfile your package manager regenerated. It used to have no way to carry the lockfile into a new pull request. [→](/platform/application-page#security)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a server type the provider no longer sells" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a server type the provider no longer sells" }}>
**A server type the provider no longer sells** - a pipeline step waiting for a node now says when the provider refused it because it no longer sells the node group's server type in that location (`provider_server_type_unavailable`), and how to move on. While it lasts, a build that declares no resources is sized to the room the cluster's nodes have, instead of waiting for a node that will not come. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#diagnosis-codes)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ankra Cloud control planes start on 4 vCPU and 8 GiB" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ankra Cloud control planes start on 4 vCPU and 8 GiB" }}>
**Ankra Cloud control planes** - in closed beta: the create form now starts an Ankra Cloud cluster's control plane and etcd nodes on `starter-4c-8g` (4 vCPU, 8 GiB) where the zone offers it, because on 2 vCPU and 4 GiB the Kubernetes API can run out of memory and stall. Any plan the zone offers can still be chosen. [→](/guides/ankra-cloud-clusters#create-the-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines"]} description="Also shipped this week: the base commit in every step" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: the base commit in every step" }}>
**The base commit in every step** - every step now carries `ANKRA_BASE_SHA`, the commit the tested change sits on: the target branch's tip for a pull request tested as merged, and the car ahead in a merge train. It is empty for a push, a schedule or a manual run. [→](/guides/pipeline-reference#what-every-step-carries)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Security", "AI"]} rss={{ title: "Ask Ankra finds the newer tag that fixes an image's vulnerability, and every place the image is declared" }}>
## Ask Ankra finds the newer tag that fixes an image's vulnerability, and every place the image is declared

For an add-on's or a third-party image, Ask Ankra names the smallest step that carries the fix (the newest tag of the nearest release line) and the newest release. Each is pinned as `tag@sha256` and marked as carrying the fix, still affected or not scanned. It also lists every stack that declares the image, with its cluster, the GitOps file and line where there is one, and how to change it. Both are also MCP tools: `find_image_fix_candidates` and `find_image_declarations`.

[When no application of yours builds the image →](/guides/fix-a-vulnerable-image#when-no-application-of-yours-builds-the-image)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: an application in another organisation" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: an application in another organisation" }}>
**An application in another organisation** - a link to an application that is not in the organisation you are in now says so, with a button for up to six of your other organisations to look there. When you have more, **All your organisations** opens the full list. It used to say the application "couldn't be checked" and offer Try again, which never helped. [→](/platform/find-your-way-around#your-organisation-and-your-account)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: manifests your repository already has" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: manifests your repository already has" }}>
**Manifests your repository already has** - running an application's setup again now keeps each manifest under `.ankra/manifests/` exactly as committed, changing only an image that moved registries or missing database variables. It used to replace a hand-tuned Deployment with Ankra's template. [→](/pipelines/generated-pipelines#when-your-repository-already-has-manifests)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: running setup again" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: running setup again" }}>
**A stale setup pull request** - running setup again now takes back what an earlier run put in the open setup pull request and no longer proposes, and closes it when nothing else is left. A file someone else changed stays unless still proposed, a reopened pull request is not closed again or emptied, and a branch with unmerged work is never reset. [→](/pipelines/generated-pipelines#when-setup-runs-again)

**Setup pull requests that only update** - a setup pull request that only amends manifests your repository already has is now titled "Update Kubernetes manifests", not "Add". [→](/pipelines/generated-pipelines#when-setup-runs-again)

**Another organisation on your repository** - setup now says when another Ankra organisation also has an application on the same private repository and branch, without saying which. [→](/pipelines/generated-pipelines#when-another-organisation-sets-up-the-same-repository)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: pipeline messages" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: pipeline messages" }}>
**Pipeline validation** - a `checkout`, `build` or `scan` stage that inherits `network: "none"` from `defaults.network` now gets a warning naming the source. It used to pass as `ok`, and the build then failed on its first `FROM`. [→](/guides/pipeline-reference#the-tier-a-stage-gets-by-default)

**Stranded steps** - a step that never started before its run's time budget ran out now says what it waited on. A busy CI pool names CI capacity instead of the cluster's agent. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#when-something-fails)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a yes keeps the conversation's tools" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a yes keeps the conversation's tools" }}>
**Saying yes to Ankra AI** - a short reply such as "yes" to a change Ankra AI proposed now keeps the tools the conversation had. On a cluster with more than 15 stacks, its summary of the cluster now names or counts the stacks it does not detail. It used to answer that the stack it had just proposed to change did not exist. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ankra AI changes in the audit log" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ankra AI changes in the audit log" }}>
**Ankra AI changes in the audit log** - pressing Stop while Ankra AI makes a change with a tool that never raises a card, such as a ticket comment or a stack draft edit, no longer loses its audit entry. The change was made, but the entry could be lost. [→](/security/audit-export#what-the-export-contains)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters"]} description="Also shipped this week: every kind of event on a cluster's Metrics" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: every kind of event on a cluster's Metrics" }}>
**Events on a cluster's Metrics** - the charts now also draw node readiness and alert firings, and sort the cluster's operations into **Changes**, **Nodes** and **Ankra's checks**, which start hidden. Pointing at a mark also says who started it when Ankra knows. Until now every event drawn was a change. [→](/platform/cluster-metrics#events-across-the-charts)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["Clusters"]} description="Also shipped this week: a playground without a website" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a playground without a website" }}>
**A playground without a website** - while a playground starts, its page now says the starter website is optional and offers **Go straight to my cluster**, which opens the playground's own page while it keeps starting. [→](/get-started/playground#start-a-playground-and-put-a-website-on-it)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-23" tags={["AI", "Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Pause Ankra AI insights on one cluster, and see only its tickets" }}>
## Pause Ankra AI insights on one cluster, and see only its tickets

A cluster's **Settings → Ankra AI** page can now pause Ankra AI's health check for an hour, 8 hours, a day, a week or until a date up to 90 days ahead. It starts again by itself, or you can turn it off until someone turns it back on. The same step can close the open insights there and their tickets. **Tickets for this cluster** opens the AI board showing only that cluster's tickets.

[Pause or leave out one cluster →](/platform/ai-insights#pause-or-leave-out-one-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "You can open, follow and close support cases inside Ankra Cloud, with screenshots" }}>
## You can open, follow and close support cases inside Ankra Cloud, with screenshots

**Support** in the Ankra Cloud menu now lists your support cases, and **Contact Ankra** opens a form for a subject, what happened and its impact. You choose whether to include the page you came from. Reply, close or reopen a case and attach up to four screenshots, all inside Cloud. It used to send you on to Ankra Support. An account owner can transfer cases from before linking to the linked organisation once staff approve.

[Support inside Ankra Cloud →](/platform/support#support-inside-ankra-cloud)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: steps that did not work" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: steps that did not work" }}>
**Steps that did not work** - in Ask Ankra, a group of steps now shows a cross when none of its steps worked and a warning when some did not. It used to show a green check over steps that had failed. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: steps that never started" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: steps that never started" }}>
**Steps that never started** - a step whose pod ended before its container started now fails as Ankra's, `agent_infra`, with a message that starts "This step never ran" and says how far the pod got, and Ankra runs it once more. It used to read as your step failing, `step_failed` with "the step failed", and was not retried. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#when-something-fails)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: alerts that clear on their own" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: alerts that clear on their own" }}>
**Alerts that clear on their own** - when an alert stops firing while its fix waits for approval, the ticket and the approval's Slack channel now say so, with the time. A change applied after the alert cleared is not credited as the fix: the ticket closes `unverifiable`, with a note that starts **Resolved before the fix**. [→](/platform/ai-board#when-the-alert-clears-before-the-change)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["GitOps", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: GitOps files you edited" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: GitOps files you edited" }}>
**GitOps files you edited** - a file you changed in Git and later brought back in line with Ankra keeps your comments and layout. Ankra writes its next change to it only when nothing you wrote is lost; otherwise the file is kept for you to decide, where it used to be overwritten. [→](/concepts/gitops#diverged-files)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: stopped clusters" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: stopped clusters" }}>
**Stopped clusters** - removing an application's deployment from a stopped cluster is now recorded and carried out when the cluster starts, instead of the start bringing it back. Until then, the API's `withdraw-removal` route takes it back. A start during which another change to the cluster is saved answers that the cluster is still stopped: start it again. [→](/concepts/applications#remove-a-deployment-from-one-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: backup vault routing" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: backup vault routing" }}>
**Velero restore points** stop before creating a backup when the cluster's configured storage location differs from the requested vault. [→](/guides/backups#take-a-restore-point-now)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Changing a Kubernetes object opens a window on that one action, with Copy beside the name you type" }}>
## Changing a Kubernetes object opens a window on that one action, with Copy beside the name you type

A resource's page has a button per action: **Restart…**, **Edit manifest…**, **Reveal values…** and **Delete…**. Each opens a window saying where the object comes from and what the action does to it, such as a Job's CronJob keeping its schedule. When a Stack owns the object, the window says why it is not changed here and opens the Stack. Most typed confirmations in Ankra now have **Copy** beside the name.

[Change a resource →](/platform/kubernetes-insights#change-a-resource)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["AI", "Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Ask Ankra and MCP clients can disconnect an imported cluster" }}>
## Ask Ankra and MCP clients can disconnect an imported cluster

Asking Ankra, or an MCP client such as Claude Code, to remove an imported cluster used to end in a refusal that only Ankra-built clusters can be deleted. The new `disconnect_cluster` tool does what **Disconnect cluster** does: it removes the cluster from Ankra and revokes its agent, changes nothing inside the cluster, and works while the agent is offline. It needs `clusters.delete` and the cluster's name typed to confirm.

[Terminate or remove →](/platform/cluster-settings#terminate-or-remove)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: tool names in Ankra AI replies" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: tool names in Ankra AI replies" }}>
**Tool names in Ankra AI replies** - when a reply names one of Ankra AI's tools, it now writes the tool's name in words, such as **Workspace Bump Dependency**. It used to write "an internal tool" in its place, so a reply such as "I'll rerun an internal tool for nanoid" did not say what Ankra would do. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: dependency bumps" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: dependency bumps" }}>
**Dependency bumps** - a security fix or an Ankra AI bump of an npm, pnpm or yarn lockfile now runs in the repository's workspace profile that carries the package manager, such as `node` beside a Go `default`. It used to run only in `default`, and was refused "pnpm is not in the workspace image". [→](/guides/ankra-workspaces#which-profile-a-dependency-bump-uses)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: changes the cluster moved on from" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: changes the cluster moved on from" }}>
**Out of date changes** - a change Ankra proposed that was not made because the cluster changed now stays **Out of date** after a reload, with a one-line reason, and offers **Ask Ankra to look again**. It used to ask for your approval again. [→](/guides/troubleshooting-ankra-ai#a-change-was-refused-or-never-ran)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: sizes in a committed Deployment" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: sizes in a committed Deployment" }}>
**Sizes in a committed Deployment** - an application's deployments now name each replica count, CPU or memory written into a committed Deployment that the deploy writes over, the value Ankra renders instead, and the commit `.ankra/ankra.yaml` was read at. [→](/reference/application-descriptor#changing-a-default-in-git)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["CLI", "API"]} description="Also shipped this week: following the repository default again" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: following the repository default again" }}>
**Use repository default from the CLI and MCP** - `ankra application deploy --reset <option>` and `reset_inputs` on `deploy_application` hand an environment's own value back to `.ankra/ankra.yaml`, as the deploy form does. A reset the deploy cannot honour is refused naming the option. [→](/reference/application-descriptor#changing-a-default-in-git)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "A cluster's Metrics page reads in tabs: Resources, Nodes, Namespaces, Traffic and GPUs" }}>
## A cluster's Metrics page reads in tabs: Resources, Nodes, Namespaces, Traffic and GPUs

A cluster's **Metrics** now opens on tabs, each with its numbers for the window and then its cards, with the cluster's changes drawn across them. **Nodes** says what the nodes cost on their provider, and pressing a node narrows the page to it. **Traffic** links each application to its own Metrics. **GPUs** appears when your Prometheus reads a GPU. A card names its source only when it is your Prometheus.

[Cluster metrics →](/platform/cluster-metrics#the-tabs-of-metrics)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: creating an UpCloud cluster" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: creating an UpCloud cluster" }}>
**Creating an UpCloud cluster** - when your UpCloud account's core or memory limit cannot hold the cluster's servers, Ankra now refuses the create before anything is created and gives the numbers: what the account allows, what its running servers use and what the cluster needs. Until now the create started and failed partway. An account on UpCloud's standard free trial allows 2 cores and 4 GiB in total. [→](/guides/upcloud-clusters#before-you-start)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "New clusters get their certificates from ZeroSSL" }}>
## New clusters get their certificates from ZeroSSL

A cluster you create with the networking stack now gets its certificates from ZeroSSL. Let's Encrypt issues 50 new certificates a week for all of `ankra.cc`, so in a busy week a new address waited for its certificate. Each new cluster registers its own ACME account at ZeroSSL with a binding Ankra holds. The issuer is still named `letsencrypt-prod`, and clusters you already have keep Let's Encrypt. A domain of yours with a CAA record has to allow `sectigo.com`.

[Certificates →](/guides/use-your-own-domain#certificates)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: support" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: support" }}>
**Support** - **Support** in the **Account** menu and in the command palette now opens the **New support case** form in the web app, and an older **Contact Ankra** bookmark lands on the same form. The form can send the page you came from, a screenshot of yours can be withdrawn from a case, and **Related cases** links a follow-up to its original case. [→](/platform/support#opening-a-case-in-the-web-app)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: removing a cluster" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: removing a cluster" }}>
**Terminate or remove** - a cluster's **Settings → Terminate or remove** page shows each way out as one card with one button. **Force terminate cluster** is a quieter button under **Terminate cluster**, for when terminating fails or gets stuck, and **Stop the cluster instead** shows only while the cluster is running. [→](/platform/cluster-settings#terminate-or-remove)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: adding an application" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: adding an application" }}>
**Adding an application** - when GitHub briefly refuses a file Ankra has just written to a new repository, Ankra now tries again, so the setup pull request opens. Until now the setup stopped with "Could not open the setup pull request" and a GitHub 422, and you had to run it again yourself. [→](/get-started/put-your-app-online#what-can-go-wrong)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: running an approved plan" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: running an approved plan" }}>
**Running an approved plan** - Ask Ankra no longer stops partway through an approved plan with **Ankra stopped before it finished**. It now carries on through the plan. [→](/guides/troubleshooting-ankra-ai#the-answer-stopped-before-it-finished)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: staged add-on upgrades" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: staged add-on upgrades" }}>
**Staged add-on upgrades** - a cluster Ankra could not read keeps its place in a fleet upgrade, and the steps after it stay locked until it reads again. Until now they could go ahead without waiting for it. No add-on upgrade can be approved while Ankra cannot read which clusters are online, and **All at once** waits while a step you just approved is still rolling. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Cost", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: the cost overview" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: the cost overview" }}>
**Cost overview** - in closed beta, the month's figures carry one line that says why a total is **At least**, such as **8 of 25 clusters have no price**, with the clusters and hours under **What is left out**. It replaces up to five separate notes. When only estimated savings wait, the savings figure shows the estimate. [→](/platform/cloud-cost#in-the-portal-closed-beta)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a pull request's place in the merge train" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a pull request's place in the merge train" }}>
**Place in the merge train** - Ask Ankra and MCP clients now give a pull request's place in the merge train, 1 for the first car. They used to read an internal ordering number as the place, so a first car could be called 1368th in the queue. [→](/pipelines/merge-train#put-a-pull-request-on-the-train)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: filing a support case" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: filing a support case" }}>
**Filing a support case** - opening a Platform support case from the web app, `ankra support create` or Ankra AI works again. Since early on 22 November each attempt was refused with **invalid Support request**, and no case was opened. [→](/platform/support#where-to-open-a-ticket)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: deleting a GitHub credential" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: deleting a GitHub credential" }}>
**Deleting a GitHub credential** - deleting a credential that comes from the Ankra GitHub App no longer answers **Failed to delete github credential from secret store, please contact support**. The credential had been deleted all the same; the message was wrong. [→](/platform/credentials#delete-a-credential)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-22" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: playgrounds" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: playgrounds" }}>
**Starting a playground** - when Ankra Cloud has no room for a new playground in its zone, the start now ends within a few minutes and says so, instead of after 35 minutes. Its status offers another zone only when Ankra has one. [→](/guides/cluster-sandbox#if-it-could-not-be-started)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: bastions" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: bastions" }}>
**Bastions** - Ankra now finds and corrects the second default route that made SSH to an UpCloud bastion drop at random. On some bastions the correction takes effect at the bastion's next restart. An UpCloud bastion that accepts connections but never answers SSH is now restarted automatically after an hour. [→](/platform/cluster-nodes#restart-a-machine-or-resize-the-bastion)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "A Platform support case opens on its latest reply, and you can add screenshots when you reply" }}>
## A Platform support case opens on its latest reply, and you can add screenshots when you reply

A support case under **Support** now shows the latest reply first, under **Latest public update**, then your **Original request**; older messages are under **Earlier replies**. A line beside the status says where the case stands, such as **Ankra is waiting for your reply**. Choose, drop or paste screenshots when you reply and remove any before you send: PNG, JPEG, WebP or GIF, up to four per case. A closed case offers **Open a follow-up** at the top.

[The support case page →](/platform/support#the-support-case-page)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "Connecting Claude Code to Ankra is one paste, then you pick what it does first" }}>
## Connecting Claude Code to Ankra is one paste, then you pick what it does first

On **Add Ankra to your agent**, the command for Claude Code now signs in too: its second line opens Ankra in your browser, and the page says what to do next. The page opens on **Ankra CLI**, whose one command also offers Ankra's skills and a connection for Claude Code. Once an agent is connected, you pick what it does first: a playground, your app, your own cluster, or a look around. **Set up** lists what is connected.

[Connect your AI agent →](/get-started/connect-your-ai-agent#connect-the-agent)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "A plan in Ask Ankra names the cluster each step runs on, and says why a step lists no changes" }}>
## A plan in Ask Ankra names the cluster each step runs on, and says why a step lists no changes

One plan can change several clusters, and the plan card now names each step's cluster under its text, as **On ankra-build ·**. Approving the plan runs each step on the cluster it names. A step's changes are worked out against what is deployed on that cluster, and a step with no list says why: nothing differs from what is deployed, it runs on a cluster an earlier step creates, or its changes cannot be listed before it runs.

[Choose a mode →](/platform/ai-assistant#choose-a-mode)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra has its own sign-in and sign-up pages" }}>
## Ankra has its own sign-in and sign-up pages

Signing in at `platform.ankra.app` now starts on Ankra's own page. Choose **Google**, **Apple**, **GitHub** or **Microsoft** to go straight to that provider, or enter your email address and continue with your password. A work address on a domain with single sign-on is offered your organisation's sign-in. The provider you used last on this browser is listed first, and **Create an account** offers the same choices. The second step and the email verification page have the same design.

[Account security and two-step sign-in →](/platform/account-security#the-sign-in-page)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Pipelines", "API"]} rss={{ title: "A default you change in .ankra/ankra.yaml reaches your environments on their next deploy" }}>
## A default you change in .ankra/ankra.yaml reaches your environments on their next deploy

Every deploy now reads `.ankra/ankra.yaml` at the commit it deploys and renders with the defaults it declares, so raising `memory_limit` in Git resizes each environment on its next deploy without running setup again. A value set with `--set` or in the deploy form stays that environment's own, and the application's deployments list it beside the default it replaces.

[Changing a default in Git →](/reference/application-descriptor#changing-a-default-in-git)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra Cloud prices: public IPv4 €2.00, block storage from €0.05, backups €0.02, hourly over 672 hours" }}>
## Ankra Cloud prices: public IPv4 €2.00, block storage from €0.05, backups €0.02, hourly over 672 hours

A public IPv4 address on Ankra Cloud now costs €2.00 a month, down from €4.00, wherever it is held. Block storage costs €0.05 (HDD), €0.08 (Local NVMe) and €0.11 (Ankra Storage) per GiB-month, and backups and snapshots €0.02. An hourly price is now the monthly price divided by 672, so a resource that runs all month reaches its monthly cap after 28 days. Volumes you already have are billed at the new prices from the hour the change shipped.

[Ankra Cloud billing →](https://cloud.ankra.app/docs/billing)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: the pipeline queue" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: the pipeline queue" }}>
**Queue order at the run limit** - when every slot under **Runs at the same time** is taken, queued runs take turns across your repositories, so one repository's burst of pull request runs no longer holds every slot. Default-branch pushes and merge train cars still start first, and a re-run waits by its original run's queue time. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#capacity-and-concurrency)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Chart your own metrics on a card of an application's dashboard" }}>
## Chart your own metrics on a card of an application's dashboard

On a dashboard of an application's **Metrics**, **Add a card** now lists the metrics the Prometheus, VictoriaMetrics or Grafana Cloud connected to its cluster holds for the application's namespace. Pick one, choose how to read it, such as per second or as a percentile of a histogram, what to split it by and the card's title, and see a preview before you add it. With no source connected, **Connect your own metrics** opens the cluster's dialog to connect one.

[Chart your own metrics →](/platform/application-page#chart-your-own-metrics)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} description="Also shipped this week: managed service setup" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: managed service setup" }}>
**Managed PostgreSQL setup** - in closed beta: where the organisation has backups enabled, a setup's review now says before you confirm whether the service will be backed up, and why not when it will not. With several verified backup vaults and none named, the review used to say nothing and the service came up without backups. Shown by `ankra services setup` from CLI 0.84.0 and carried as `backups` in the API's plan. [→](/concepts/managed-services#limits-and-good-to-know)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: approving stack changes" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: approving stack changes" }}>
**Approving a stack change** - a change Ankra proposed to a stack's add-ons or manifests no longer comes back **Out of date** when you approve it because another stack on the same cluster changed meanwhile, such as a Git sync or an application deploy. Only a change to that stack, on the cluster or in its draft, makes it out of date now. An `expected_digest` sent with a stack update through the API or CLI is checked the same way. [→](/guides/troubleshooting-ankra-ai#a-change-was-refused-or-never-ran)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: add-ons on a stopped cluster" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: add-ons on a stopped cluster" }}>
**Add-ons on a stopped cluster** - upgrading or rolling back an add-on whose cluster is stopped, from a security fix, the API or Ankra AI, now says "The cluster is stopped, so this add-on cannot change. Start the cluster first." (`409`, `ADDON_CLUSTER_STOPPED`). It used to answer "Addon not found in cluster", as if the add-on were gone. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application secrets" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application secrets" }}>
**Secrets** - on an application's **Variables**, a secret's value now stays masked while you type it in a box of several lines too: in **Add secret** with **Multiline input**, in **Import…** and in a shared default's **New value**. **Reveal** shows it. Those boxes used to show it as typed. **Import…** also takes a file of `KEY=value` lines, and adds them after what the box holds. [→](/platform/application-page#secrets)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: hosted logs" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: hosted logs" }}>
**Hosted log levels** - on Ankra's hosted logs, the bars on an application's **Logs** and the count above them put a line under the level its text states, such as `"level":"error"` or `level=warn`. Every such line used to count under no level, so the count showed no errors or warnings. [→](/platform/application-page#which-level-a-line-counts-under)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: connecting a cluster" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: connecting a cluster" }}>
**Connecting a cluster** - the wait before **No connection yet** counts from any copy of the install command, with **Copy** or by hand, and a reload keeps it. With no copy on record, as on a page opened again later, it counts from when the cluster was added. [→](/guides/import-cluster#when-it-does-not-connect)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-21" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: ways to sign in" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: ways to sign in" }}>
**Ways to sign in** - **Add a way to sign in**, then **Microsoft**, now goes straight to Microsoft's sign-in instead of the page that lists every method. [→](/platform/account-security#add-a-way-to-sign-in)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Restore a managed PostgreSQL service into a new service, as of a moment you choose" }}>
## Restore a managed PostgreSQL service into a new service, as of a moment you choose

In closed beta: a backed-up PostgreSQL service can be restored into a new service, as of a moment you choose or the newest point its backups reach. The original is not changed, so you can check the data first, then connect your applications with the new `ankra services connect`. Available in the CLI (`ankra services setup --from`), the API and MCP. The portal has no restore action yet.

[Restore a managed PostgreSQL service →](/guides/restore-a-managed-postgresql-service)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: managed PostgreSQL" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: managed PostgreSQL" }}>
**Managed PostgreSQL backups** - in closed beta: a PostgreSQL service set up with backups on had no backup until its stack was protected by hand. Ankra now switches the backups on by itself within a few minutes of setup, and restarts the database once to do it. For a service set up before 14:00 UTC on 20 November 2026, take a restore point once with `ankra cluster stacks restore-points create <service>`, which switches its backups on. [→](/concepts/managed-services#limits-and-good-to-know)

**Deleting a managed PostgreSQL service** - in closed beta: a PostgreSQL service with a restore point could not be deleted. Its stack was removed, then the namespace and the data stayed as **Data not confirmed deleted**, because Ankra counted its own copies of the backup storage credentials as another workload. A deletion now removes the namespace as its review says. For a service left in that state, review the deletion again. [→](/guides/delete-a-managed-service#when-a-deletion-stops-short)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Save a search on an application's Logs and run it again from Saved searches" }}>
## Save a search on an application's Logs and run it again from Saved searches

On an application's **Logs**, **Saved searches** is a new menu in the row under the search box. **Save this search** keeps the words, the level, the copy and the time range under a name, and picking the name runs the search again. The searches belong to the application, so everyone who can see it can run them. Saving, renaming and removing one need permission to change the application.

[Saved searches →](/platform/application-page#saved-searches)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: the first screen" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: the first screen" }}>
**First screen** - **Skip to Home** and the setup wizard's **I'll do this later** now open Home even when your playground is ordered at that moment. A press made just then used to save the choice and leave you on the same page. [→](/get-started/start-here#coming-back-to-the-first-screen)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Pipelines", "AI"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ask Ankra and the merge train" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ask Ankra and the merge train" }}>
**Merge train** - on a repository with a merge train where merge when green is not available, ask Ask Ankra to merge a pull request and it proposes putting the pull request in the merge train. Approve the card instead of running `ankra pipeline train add` yourself. [→](/pipelines/merge-train#put-a-pull-request-on-the-train)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: nodes that never joined" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: nodes that never joined" }}>
**Nodes** - a server that never became a node, because Kubernetes was never set up on it, now fails its update once as **Machine never joined the cluster**, instead of failing every day. [→](/guides/cluster-will-not-come-up#what-can-go-wrong)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Ask to add your own dedicated servers to an Ankra Cloud private cloud, as a single or a highly available region" }}>
## Ask to add your own dedicated servers to an Ankra Cloud private cloud, as a single or a highly available region

**Private cloud** in the Ankra Cloud console now takes a request for dedicated servers you rent or own, such as OVHcloud's. Describe them and the network between them, then choose **Single** or **Highly available**. Highly available keeps three copies of every volume and needs three or more servers in one data centre on a dedicated private network of 10 Gbit/s or more. Anything else is offered as Single, and the form says why. The API field is `own_servers`.

[Single or highly available →](https://cloud.ankra.app/docs/concepts/bring-your-own-servers#single-or-highly-available)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ankra Cloud servers and storage" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ankra Cloud servers and storage" }}>
**Rebuild and restore** - rebuilding a server, or restoring a backup in place, answers `409` while the storage has snapshots. Both used to erase the snapshots and keep listing them. [→](https://cloud.ankra.app/docs/concepts/storage-and-backups)

**Kubernetes node removal** - a node pool that scales down, rolls or replaces a node deletes the node's server only after its volumes have detached. A PersistentVolume still attached used to be deleted with the node. [→](https://cloud.ankra.app/docs/kubernetes-csi)

**Detach** - a storage detached from a server is attached to another only once the first server has released it. Until then the attach answers `409`. [→](https://cloud.ankra.app/docs/concepts/storage-and-backups)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: backups" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: backups" }}>
**Stacks in a variable namespace** - a stack whose add-on or manifest names its namespace with a variable, such as `${{ ankra.stack_name }}`, was read as holding no data. Protecting it (`ankra cluster stacks protect`, or **Save policy** on the Backups page) turned protection on with nothing to back up, and the backup posture said **unprotected**. Ankra now reads the namespace as it is deployed. If you protected such a stack before, protect it again or take a restore point by hand. [→](/guides/backups#troubleshooting)

**PostgreSQL backups into an UpCloud vault** - the database's uploads were refused with `XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch`, so its restore points failed. A database you protect now uploads. For one protected before, protect the stack again and restart its instances once. [→](/guides/backups#troubleshooting)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application dashboards" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application dashboards" }}>
**Dashboards** - an application's dashboard is called a dashboard on its **Metrics** and in Ask Ankra. Its messages on **Metrics** say dashboard, such as **This dashboard changed**, and the panel beside a conversation is headed **Dashboard** while everything on it is an application's dashboard. Both said canvas. [→](/platform/application-page#make-a-dashboard)

**Response time p95** - on an Overview chart card, a time when more than 5 in 100 requests took longer than the highest bound the cluster's ingress measures is a gap, and the line under the chart says how many such times there were and names the bound. The card used to draw the bound as the p95. [→](/platform/application-page#a-chart-as-a-card)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: pipeline secrets, backups and data migration" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: pipeline secrets, backups and data migration" }}>
**Pipeline secrets** - a step that declares a secret whose name has a plain value's shared default fails with that reason and the way out: keep the name as a secret, or take it out of the step's secrets. It used to say the application had no env secret of that name. [→](/platform/application-page#plain-values)

**Backups** - `get_application_backups`, in Ankra AI and over MCP, now returns what each deployment's backups keep, as the application's Backups shows it: how long the policy keeps restore points, how many are kept now and the oldest, since when it is protected and its last restore test. [→](/platform/mcp-tools#backups-%26-restore-points)

**Data migration** - the Launch Plan, read through the API or with `get_application_configuration` in Ankra AI and over MCP, warns about each environment that keeps its own value of a variable the cutover changes, which it goes on reading after the cutover, and each run names them too. [→](/use-cases/vercel-to-ankra#moving-your-data-closed-beta)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["GitOps", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Inspect every recorded preserved-file difference" }}>
## Inspect every recorded preserved-file difference

CLI 0.80.0 adds `ankra cluster gitops preserved show <path> --all` to read
every recorded structural difference, with the same masking and role checks.
It returns no partial result if the snapshot changes. Resolving a truncated
preview first reads the complete matching differences; an incomplete read
prevents the decision, including with `--yes`. Requires a platform that serves complete differences.

[Decide a preserved file →](/concepts/gitops#diverged-files)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Launch Plan" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Launch Plan" }}>
**Launch Plan** - for an organisation that does not have managed services turned on, the plan no longer offers to run PostgreSQL, a key-value store, object storage or OpenSearch in your cluster, or a portability pull request that leads to one. Approving such a step used to end in a refusal. The app keeps the service it has, and its card says why. [→](/use-cases/vercel-to-ankra#vercel-storage-in-the-launch-plan)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "An Ankra Cloud account you created directly gets its €59 sign-up credit once you use it through the Ankra Platform" }}>
## An Ankra Cloud account you created directly gets its €59 sign-up credit once you use it through the Ankra Platform

An Ankra Cloud account signed up for directly now receives the €59 sign-up credit when it starts being used through the Ankra Platform: when you connect it to your organisation, or when the Platform first works in it for you. The 30 days run from that moment. Each account receives the sign-up credit once.

[Sign-up credit on Ankra Cloud →](/platform/billing#sign-up-credit-on-ankra-cloud)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: billing" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: billing" }}>
**Applications** - when you apply for a credit programme or monthly invoicing, a notification and an email now confirm that it arrived, and you are emailed again when it is decided. [→](/platform/billing#credit)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Make a dashboard of an application and add its cards, on its Metrics" }}>
## Make a dashboard of an application and add its cards, on its Metrics

On an application's **Metrics**, **All dashboards** now has **New dashboard**, which makes an empty dashboard of the application that only you see until you share it. **Ask Ankra for a dashboard** starts a conversation with Ankra AI about the application instead. On a dashboard's tab, **Add a card** lists what Ankra measures about the application, such as CPU used or Response time p95, and puts the one you pick on the dashboard as a chart.

[Make a dashboard →](/platform/application-page#make-a-dashboard)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["AI", "Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra AI finds server records whose machine is gone and removes them once you approve" }}>
## Ankra AI finds server records whose machine is gone and removes them once you approve

Ask Ankra and the MCP server have two new tools. `get_cluster_machine_inventory` compares a cluster's server records with its provider and its Kubernetes nodes, and names each record whose machine is gone. `retire_gone_machine_rows` removes those records once you approve, and only when the provider confirms the machine is gone. Such a record used to keep counting toward its node group. It works on Hetzner, UpCloud, DigitalOcean, AWS and Ankra Cloud clusters.

[MCP tool reference →](/platform/mcp-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "New applications are sized for their runtime and stay safe to drain with more than one replica" }}>
## New applications are sized for their runtime and stay safe to drain with more than one replica

A new application now gets CPU and memory for the runtime Ankra detects, with the memory request equal to the limit and no CPU limit. Run two or more replicas and each deploy adds a PodDisruptionBudget that lets a node drain take one replica at a time, a spread across nodes (and across zones where the nodes span them), and zone-local routing on clusters that support it. Existing applications keep their sizes.

[Default options →](/reference/application-descriptor#size-by-runtime)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Cost", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: adding a card" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: adding a card" }}>
**Adding a card** - anyone who may manage billing can now save a card from the **Plan** card with **Add a card**, owner or not. [→](/platform/billing#overview)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: security fixes" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: security fixes" }}>
**Security fixes** - a fleet add-on upgrade no longer reads as failed, with **Try again now** on its next step, because of a cluster that has left the plan. [→](/guides/cluster-security)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Baseline" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Baseline" }}>
**Baseline** - the PodDisruptionBudget check now also fails a budget that allows no disruption at all, because it blocks every drain of its nodes. [→](/reference/cli/cluster#ankra-cluster-baseline)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["API"]} rss={{ title: "The Ankra platform has a status page at ankra.ai/status, and a public JSON feed of the same report" }}>
## The Ankra platform has a status page at ankra.ai/status, and a public JSON feed of the same report

Ankra's own status page at ankra.ai/status shows how each of the platform's seven services is doing, open incidents and maintenance in progress with their updates, planned maintenance, and the last 30 days. Ankra staff write every entry; nothing on it is measured. Scripts and agents read the same report as JSON from `https://platform.ankra.app/api/v1/status`, with no credential, from any origin, cached for ten seconds.

[Platform status →](/platform/support#platform-status)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-20" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ask Ankra" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ask Ankra" }}>
**Ask Ankra** - when a conversation that started on no cluster moves onto one, that cluster's tools are there in its very next step, and Ankra AI is no longer told to call a tool it was not given. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "A new PostgreSQL service requires TLS and delivers its CA" }}>
## A new PostgreSQL service requires TLS and delivers its CA

In closed beta: a PostgreSQL managed service set up from now on refuses connections that are not encrypted, and each connected application receives the service's CA as `DATABASE_CA_CERT`, so a client can verify the server. `DATABASE_URL` is unchanged and most clients connect as before. An application on node-postgres or postgres.js must turn TLS on and pass the CA. A service that already runs keeps accepting the connections it accepts today. A new service needs Kubernetes 1.30 or later.

[PostgreSQL and TLS →](/guides/connect-to-a-managed-service#postgresql-and-tls)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application History, dashboards and data migration" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application History, dashboards and data migration" }}>
**History** - a value that was empty reads **Empty** in a change's **Before and after** table, and a row that sets a variable to one says so, such as **LOG_LEVEL set to empty in Staging**. Both used to leave the value blank. [→](/platform/application-page#changes-to-settings)

**History** - on a wide window, where the open change sits beside the list, each of its details keeps its value under its own label. A label could end one row with its value at the start of the next. [→](/platform/application-page#history)

**Dashboards** - a dashboard's chart of an application's measure writes it in the measure's unit: memory in MiB or GiB, CPU in cores, response time in ms or s. **Memory used** was drawn as a bare count of bytes. [→](/platform/application-page#add-a-card)

**Data migration** - starting one through the API is refused, naming the variable, while the application keeps a variable the cutover changes as a plain value's shared default. A plain value added after the start fails the cutover, which rolls back and makes the vendor sources writable again. Such a run used to stay stuck with the sources read-only. [→](/use-cases/vercel-to-ankra#moving-your-data-closed-beta)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines", "Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Response time is drawn as p50, p95 and p99, with a grid of how requests spread" }}>
## Response time is drawn as p50, p95 and p99, with a grid of how requests spread

On an application's **Metrics**, the **Response time** card on **Traffic** now draws p50, p95 and p99 on one chart, where it drew p95 alone. Its **Lines** / **Spread** switch shows a grid instead: the share of each moment's requests in each band of response time. Where p50 or p99 lies above the highest bound the ingress measures, the card says more than that bound. A cluster's **Metrics** has p50, p99 and spread charts under **Ingress traffic**.

[Response time →](/platform/application-page#response-time)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: log levels" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: log levels" }}>
**Log levels** - a dashboard's **Log volume by level** chart and the `code_context` MCP tool read a line's level as the Logs counts do, from its own level field, numbers such as pino's 50 included. A word in its message no longer makes a line that states a lower level an error or a warning. On a Loki store, the Logs counts no longer read a key such as `compression_level` as the line's level. [→](/platform/application-page#what-the-bars-count)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["AI", "Security"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra AI's bastion review can propose hardening or a restart for your approval" }}>
## Ankra AI's bastion review can propose hardening or a restart for your approval
