When AI remediation is on for a cluster, Ankra AI's review of a bastion problem can now propose `harden_bastion` or `restart_bastion`. Each waits for a person to approve it in the approvals inbox. Without AI remediation the review stays read-only and names the action for you to run from Ask Ankra.

[OS-level options →](/security/hardening#os-level-options)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ankra AI and SSH keys" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ankra AI and SSH keys" }}>
**Support tickets** - when the fix is Ankra's to make, Ask Ankra offers to raise a support ticket for you, and files it once you confirm. [→](/platform/support#from-ankra-ai-and-the-mcp-server)

**SSH keys** - a key change on a running cluster no longer stays open waiting for a stopped server whose machine the provider has deleted. Ankra removes the server's record and names it. [→](/platform/cluster-settings#ssh-access)

**Node lists** - `list_cluster_nodes` leaves deleted servers out unless you ask for them with `include_deleted`. [→](/platform/mcp-tools)

**Ask Ankra** - a conversation that began on one topic, such as a GitHub repository, can now load the tools of every area your role allows, such as stacks. It used to be limited to the areas its first question pointed at. [→](/platform/ai-assistant#skills-and-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra Cloud's €59 sign-up credit now comes only through the Ankra Platform" }}>
## Ankra Cloud's €59 sign-up credit now comes only through the Ankra Platform

The €59 sign-up credit, valid for 30 days, now goes only to the Ankra Cloud account the Ankra Platform creates for your organisation. An account signed up for on Ankra Cloud directly, with an email or with Google, Microsoft or GitHub, gets no free credit. Connect it to your organisation and the Platform's credit, its welcome credit included, pays for Ankra Cloud too. Credit already granted keeps its amount and expiry date.

[Sign-up credit on Ankra Cloud →](/platform/billing#sign-up-credit-on-ankra-cloud)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Metrics dashboards" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Metrics dashboards" }}>
**Metrics** - a dashboard's tab has a toolbar: its time range on a dashboard that reads live data, kept in the address, then **Edit layout**, **Ask Ankra to change it** and **Take off the tabs**, each for the people who may use it. [→](/platform/application-page#the-tabs-of-metrics)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "An application's Cost page shows what each environment's rate is made of" }}>
## An application's Cost page shows what each environment's rate is made of

On an application's **Cost**, each environment under **Where the money goes** now opens into the rows its monthly rate is made of: databases, workloads, storage, and jobs and other work. So does a place that belongs to no environment. Amounts are to the cent. A row Ankra has no amount for says **Not known**, never €0. When Ankra could not place part of the rate on a row, or any of it, the page says so and why.

[What a rate is made of →](/platform/application-page#what-a-rate-is-made-of)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Automatic agent upgrades turn on for older organisations on 26 November 2026" }}>
## Automatic agent upgrades turn on for older organisations on 26 November 2026

Organisations created before 7 September 2026 have automatic agent upgrades off unless someone turned them on. On 26 November 2026 the switch turns on for each one that has never changed it, and Ankra then keeps its clusters' agents on the current release. To keep a cluster's agent on its version, turn automatic upgrades off for that cluster before then, or contact support to have your organisation left as it is.

[Upgrading the agent →](/concepts/cluster-agent#upgrading-the-agent)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "An application's Metrics tabs are the same for everyone, and its dashboards can be tabs" }}>
## An application's Metrics tabs are the same for everyone, and its dashboards can be tabs

The tabs of an application's **Metrics** are now one order for everyone who opens it, kept by Ankra. A dashboard of the application shared with the organisation can be a tab, and **All dashboards** is always the last tab. People who may change the application arrange them with **Add a tab** and **Edit tabs**. Metrics opens on the tab you last picked, on any device.

[The tabs of Metrics →](/platform/application-page#the-tabs-of-metrics)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "An application's Logs bars count every line of the time you pick" }}>
## An application's Logs bars count every line of the time you pick

On an application's **Logs**, the bars and the totals above the lines are now counted by the log store over the whole time you picked, not from the lines the page has read. When there is no count, or it could not be read, the page says so and the bars count the lines read. **Load older lines** reads on from where the list ended, and the time range goes back only as far as the store keeps lines.

[What the bars count →](/platform/application-page#what-the-bars-count)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "An application's History shows what a change to a setting changed" }}>
## An application's History shows what a change to a setting changed

In an application's **History**, a change to a variable, a secret or a setting is now a row of its own. Open it for a **Before and after** table and, for most settings, a link to the page where the value lives. A secret's value is never shown. A member confined to some projects sees that something changed in an environment outside their projects, not what.

[Changes to settings →](/platform/application-page#changes-to-settings)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "An application's Environments and Backups say where the cluster and the backup vault are" }}>
## An application's Environments and Backups say where the cluster and the backup vault are

On an application's **Environments**, the line under each environment's name now says where its cluster is, such as Helsinki, Finland. **Data → Backups** says the same of the bucket its backup vault writes to. For an imported cluster, your own hardware or a bucket you brought, the page says Ankra has no record of the place. When Ankra should know and cannot say, it reads **Place not known**, with the reason.

[Where the cluster is →](/platform/application-page#where-the-cluster-is)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "An application on an Ankra hostname redirects plain HTTP to HTTPS and sends an HSTS header" }}>
## An application on an Ankra hostname redirects plain HTTP to HTTPS and sends an HSTS header

An application Ankra publishes on an Ankra hostname now answers plain HTTP with a redirect to HTTPS, and its HTTPS responses carry an HSTS header. It applies where the cluster's own Traefik serves the Ingress and ends TLS, from the application's next deploy. A deploy that skips it because of the Ingress's class says so in its output. An Ingress with your own domain on it is not changed. Deploy with `--set ingress_https_redirect=false` to keep plain HTTP.

[Default options →](/reference/application-descriptor#default-options)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: CI clusters" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: CI clusters" }}>
**Add a cluster** - once you pick a cluster, the dialog says what adding it does before anything changes: whether it can take runs now, how many pipeline workers its agent runs or that Ankra turns two on, which nodes its steps run on, and its share of new runs at the weight you chose. A cluster with no Ankra agent cannot be added, and the dialog says so. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#before-your-first-run)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Find" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Find" }}>
**Find** - once you finish typing, the first result is the one highlighted, so `Enter` opens it. Typing "clusters" could leave a setting highlighted below the **Clusters** page. [→](/platform/command-palette#open-it)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Ankra AI and pipeline runs" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Ankra AI and pipeline runs" }}>
**Range queries** - `query_prometheus_range` over MCP and in Ankra AI now takes a `step` or `duration` given as a whole number of seconds, such as `300`, or in more than one unit, such as `3h20m`. Both used to be refused as invalid. [→](/platform/mcp-tools#observability-%26-alerts)

**Agent runs** - an agent run that watches an Ankra Pipelines run waits until the run concludes, then reads its result. It used to be given turn after turn while it waited, and never saw the result. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#chat-watches-a-run-and-a-failed-build-gets-a-fix)

**Runs that tested nothing** - when a watched run is stopped before any step tests the change, Ankra AI says the checks could not run and why, and commits nothing. It used to be asked for a fix. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#chat-watches-a-run-and-a-failed-build-gets-a-fix)

**Setup pull request fixes** - on an application that builds with Ankra Pipelines, the fix Ankra AI makes on a red setup pull request starts from the failed pipeline run and waits for the next one. It used to wait for a GitHub Actions run that never came. When the run did not fail on a build, test or image gate step, no fix starts, and the **Ankra PR shepherd** check says why. [→](/concepts/applications#the-pr-shepherd)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Charts as cards on an application's Overview" }}>
## Charts as cards on an application's Overview

**Add a card** on an application's **Overview** now lists what Ankra measures: CPU used, Memory used, Copies running, Container restarts, Response time p95 and Requests that failed. Adding one puts a chart of the last 7 days on the page, saved with the layout for everyone who opens the application. A time with no reading is a gap in the line, never a zero.

[A chart as a card →](/platform/application-page#a-chart-as-a-card)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Make an alert for one application, from its Metrics page" }}>
## Make an alert for one application, from its Metrics page

An application's **Metrics** now has an **Alerts** card. **New alert**, or **Alert** on the failed-requests or response-time chart, makes an alert that watches that application on one environment: failed requests, response time or restarts above a number for some minutes. The card also lists your organisation's rules for the cluster, when each alert last fired and who acknowledged it. A firing nobody has taken is acknowledged from its row.

[Alert on one application →](/guides/alerts#alert-on-one-application)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Stacks and secrets" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Stacks and secrets" }}>
**Secret references** - a reference in a YAML comment or a key is refused with its line number. A value with several lines is delivered whole when its reference stands in a literal block (`key: |`). [→](/guides/organisation-secrets#troubleshooting)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: managed services" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: managed services" }}>
**PostgreSQL readiness** - a managed PostgreSQL service could read **Not ready** with "nothing accepted a connection on the published service address" while its application was using it, most often on a k3s cluster. The check's pod dialled once, the moment it started, before the cluster's NetworkPolicy let it through. It now dials again for up to 15 seconds, as the checks of the other services do. [→](/concepts/managed-services#readiness)

**Launch Plan** - for an app that uses Supabase, the plan no longer offers a Supabase-compatible database run in your cluster, which Ankra does not offer yet. Approving that option used to end in a refused step. The app keeps its hosted Supabase project. [→](/concepts/managed-services#the-services)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["API", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: API" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: API" }}>
**Server errors** - while one of Ankra's database poolers restarts, token checks and other reads are tried again, and each connection is checked before it is used. A restart used to answer some API, CLI and agent requests with `500`. [→](/api-reference/introduction#errors)

**API tokens** - **API access** in your account revokes or removes several tokens at once. Tick them, or use **Remove the N that stopped working…** to clear every expired and revoked token in one step. [→](/reference/tokens#revoke-or-remove-several-tokens-at-once)

**Canvases** - each item `GET /api/v1/org/canvases` lists now has `owner_name`, who made it, and `card_count`, how many cards its draft lays out. Either is null when Ankra does not know it. [→](/api-reference/introduction)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: steps that wait for a node" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: steps that wait for a node" }}>
**Waiting steps** - a step that waits for room on the node holding its run's workspace says so on the run page: what it needs, which node, and what ends the wait. The wait now ends. A step blocked by the cluster's own workloads is sent at once and fails within about a minute. One blocked by other runs is sent after 15 minutes at the latest. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#a-step-that-waits-for-the-node-of-its-run)

**Refused node requests** - on a cluster where Ankra autoscales a node group, the reason a node was refused stays on the run page until a later request is accepted, for up to 30 minutes. It could go when the autoscaler asked again, even if that request was refused too. A node refused for the trial limit now says what lifts the limit. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#diagnosis-codes)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application cost" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application cost" }}>
**Cost** - **For a million requests** says what the environment the application serves from, its production one when it has one, cost for its requests over the last 7 whole days. [→](/platform/application-page#cost)

**Park or remove** - each running place says what it would cost parked. [→](/platform/application-page#settings)

**Budgets** - **Cost** and the Overview's **Cost** card read each environment's budget the same way and compare it by one rule, so they never disagree on over or under budget. [→](/platform/application-page#cost)

**This month so far** - on **Cost**, it is what the month's full days cost, to the cent, with today left out. [→](/platform/application-page#cost)

**Daily spend** - on the Overview's **Cost** card, the part of a day over what the budgets allow is drawn in the warning colour. Pointing at a day says how far over or under it is. [→](/platform/application-page#cost)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application pages" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application pages" }}>
**Metrics** - a tab pressed just after you pick an environment or a time range keeps the pick, as does a run row on **Pipelines** after a filter. [→](/platform/application-page#metrics)

**Metrics** - when Ankra missed part of either period you compare, **Requests** and **Requests that failed** say so and are not compared. [→](/platform/application-page#metrics)

**Overview** - a memory chart card's axis steps are round in MiB, or GiB from 1,024 MiB, and lines and bars write their days the same way. [→](/platform/application-page#a-chart-as-a-card)

**Application header** - on a phone 320 px wide, **Open** *application* takes a row of its own and cuts no letter. A long version in a badge beside the title wraps, so the page never scrolls sideways. [→](/platform/application-page)

**Environments** - **Running** says what each copy may use at most, such as 3 of 3 copies, 512 MiB each at most. [→](/platform/application-page#environments)

**Backups** - on **Data**, a schedule's time says UTC, such as Every day at 02:00 UTC. [→](/platform/application-page#data)

**Backups** - **Restore points** says when a restore was last tested, each point has a **Restore test**, and **Kept for** says what is kept now. [→](/platform/application-page#data)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application backups, schedules, Logs and History" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application backups, schedules, Logs and History" }}>
**Backups** - the Overview's **Backups** card and **Database** on **Data** say a schedule with its time in UTC, such as every day at 02:17 UTC, where they showed `17 2 * * *`. [→](/platform/application-page#data)

**Pipelines** - on **Schedules**, **In the pipeline file, not running** says each schedule's time is read in the time zone you pick when you add it. [→](/platform/application-page#pipelines)

**Service goals** - **Keep backups for** is now checked against what your backups keep now. Where weekly or monthly copies leave the oldest restore point kept short of the goal, it says when that one is from, and judges nothing. [→](/platform/application-page#settings)

**Logs** - on Ankra's hosted logs, the bars are counted for a range that does not start on a bar's edge. Such a range could not be counted once the store held lines. [→](/platform/application-page#what-the-bars-count)

**Logs** - on an Elasticsearch or OpenSearch store, **Load older lines** no longer skips lines written in the same millisecond as the last line shown. [→](/platform/application-page#logs)

**History** - saving the value a setting already has adds no row for **Push to deploy**, **Who runs it**, **Database backups**, a deployment's backup settings, or **Turn off here** on a secret already off. [→](/platform/application-page#changes-to-settings)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-19" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Kubernetes resources" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Kubernetes resources" }}>
**Deleting Jobs** - a Job deleted through Ankra, from the web app, the CLI or an AI tool, takes its pods with it, as `kubectl delete` does. Its pods used to stay on the cluster with no owner. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights#change-a-resource)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: recovery" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: recovery" }}>
**Volume recovery** - copying volume data keeps captured application containers, init containers and restore hooks stopped. Temporary recovery Pods are removed afterward; deploying the reviewed destination draft starts the application. [→](/guides/clone-stack#review-and-deploy-the-draft-from-the-cli)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Every page of an application is redrawn, with a Scaling tab in Settings" }}>
## Every page of an application is redrawn, with a Scaling tab in Settings

All of an application's pages have a new layout. After a release that did not go live, every page says which version is still serving, and the menu counts serious findings beside **Security**. **Environments** has one card per environment with its own buttons, and **Settings** has a new **Scaling** tab for the copies, memory and CPU of each environment.

[The application page →](/platform/application-page)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: applications" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: applications" }}>
**Plain values** - an environment copied with its secret overrides gets the source's plain values too, and `list_application_env_secrets` lists an application's shared plain values. [→](/guides/application-environments#add-an-environment-on-another-cluster)

**Environments** - each environment is a card with its cluster, its version and commit, and its own buttons: **Promote to** an environment, **Deploy a version**, **Roll back to** a version, **Park** and **Open**, or **Wake** when it is parked. [→](/platform/application-page#environments)

**Environments** - the page's header has **Add an environment**, and **Recent releases to** an environment marks the version it runs **Live now**. [→](/platform/application-page#environments)

**Promotion review** - shows the version on each side with **Live there since** and **Replaces**. [→](/platform/application-page#environments)

**Pipelines** - **Runs** starts with where the default branch is and which version each environment runs. A run the image gate stopped reads **Blocked**. [→](/platform/application-page#pipelines)

**Previews** - a preview's card says when the newest run of its pull request did not pass, and which run the preview still shows. [→](/platform/application-page#previews)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application History" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application History" }}>
**History** - runs on a timer that passed are counted under the list, and **Show them** lists them. [→](/platform/application-page#history)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application Metrics and Architecture" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application Metrics and Architecture" }}>
**Metrics** - **Compare with** draws the period before on the **Traffic** charts, and the **Releases** chip shows or hides the releases on them. [→](/platform/application-page#metrics)

**Metrics** - **Runtime** lists **The copies running now**, each with its version, when it started and its restarts. **Add a tab** puts back a tab taken off. [→](/platform/application-page#metrics)

**Architecture** - **Parts and connections** now sorts the parts into **Entry points**, **Services**, **Workloads** and **Storage**, and **Source components** lists the image each component runs. [→](/platform/application-page#architecture)

**Menu** - **Variables** is marked when the next promotion waits for a secret its target does not have. [→](/platform/application-page#the-menu)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "The Cache tab says what your pipeline caches save" }}>
## The Cache tab says what your pipeline caches save

An application's **Pipelines → Cache** tab now opens with the time its caches saved over the last 30 days, and what a run takes with every cache cold beside a typical one. Each cache is shown by its name in words, such as **pnpm store**, with a **Saves** figure measured from the stage's own warm and cold runs. Under the list, the newest run that ran with every cache cold says why.

[What a cache saves →](/pipelines/caching#what-a-cache-saves)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "One Overview layout for everyone who opens an application, with versions" }}>
## One Overview layout for everyone who opens an application, with versions

An application's **Overview** can be arranged. **Edit layout** moves the cards, sets their width, takes one off or adds one, and saves the result for everyone who opens the application. Three cards are new to add: **Recent releases**, **Last pipeline runs** and **Backups**. Every saved layout is kept as a version, and **Versions** puts an earlier one back or returns to Ankra's layout.

[Arrange the Overview →](/platform/application-page#arrange-the-overview)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "A cluster's Overview shows add-on logos, links to its GitOps repository and draws 30 days of cost" }}>
## A cluster's Overview shows add-on logos, links to its GitOps repository and draws 30 days of cost

A cluster's **Overview** now shows each add-on's logo, and **Agent and GitOps** links to the cluster's folder in its GitOps repository. **What it costs** draws the last 30 days of daily spend. On **Workloads and resources** every count opens its list: Stacks, add-ons, manifests, each workload kind, services, ingresses and namespaces.

[Read a cluster's overview →](/platform/cluster-overview-and-health#2-read-the-overview-from-the-top)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "The Schedules tab says which schedules start a run" }}>
## The Schedules tab says which schedules start a run

A schedule starts a run only when the pipeline has an `on.schedule` entry with the same cron on its branch. An application's **Schedules** tab now shows which ones do. Each row says whether the pipeline file writes it. One that starts no run says why and promises no next time. Entries of the file that no schedule starts are listed under **In the pipeline file, not running**, each with **Add this schedule**.

[Run every night →](/guides/ankra-ci-starter-pipelines#run-every-night)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra hosted logs keep a cluster's container logs for 7 days, with nothing to install" }}>
## Ankra hosted logs keep a cluster's container logs for 7 days, with nothing to install

Turn on **Ankra hosted logs** for a cluster and its agent sends the log lines of the cluster's containers to Ankra, which keeps them for up to 7 days. Search them in **Cluster logs → History** and in each application's **Logs → Search**, and Ankra AI reads them when it looks into a problem. Hosted logs are off until you turn them on for a cluster, and are included during the pilot.

[Ankra hosted logs →](/guides/hosted-logs)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: hosted logs" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: hosted logs" }}>
**Hosted logs** - an agent upgrade, or a clean restart of under 15 minutes, continues each container's log from the last line sent. It used to store up to a minute of lines twice. [→](/guides/hosted-logs)

**Hosted log searches** - the note on a search of Ankra's hosted logs says the read is limited to your organisation and one cluster. It used to carry the wording for a log store that several clusters share. [→](/guides/hosted-logs)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "A cluster added to the CI pool gets its pipeline workers turned on, and any CI cluster can be taken out" }}>
## A cluster added to the CI pool gets its pipeline workers turned on, and any CI cluster can be taken out

Adding a cluster to the CI pool, choosing it as the cluster pipelines run on, or pointing a repository at it now turns on its pipeline workers when it has none. You are no longer asked to do it first. Any CI cluster can be taken out, the main one included, and another made main from its row.

[Before your first run →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#before-your-first-run)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "An application keeps plain values beside its secrets, on a view now named Variables" }}>
## An application keeps plain values beside its secrets, on a view now named Variables

An application's **Secrets** view is now **Variables**. It lists secrets and plain values in one table, each row marked **Secret** or **Plain**. A plain value is configuration that is not a credential, such as a log level: its value is shown, and one sheet holds its default and each environment's own value. **Save and apply** takes it live at once. A name that looks like a password or a key is stored only after you confirm it is plain.

[Variables →](/platform/application-page#variables)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: pipeline caches" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: pipeline caches" }}>
**Cache results** - a stage that mounted some of its cache volumes warm and created the others reports `partial`, and its line on the run page reads **Cache partly restored**. It used to report `miss`, as if the stage had started with nothing warm. [→](/pipelines/caching#did-my-cache-work)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Stacks and secrets" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Stacks and secrets" }}>
**Secret references** - a Secret manifest written as JSON, or with its secret reference in double quotes, now deploys when the stored value holds quotes of its own, such as a registry pull secret's `.dockerconfigjson`. It used to fail on every attempt with "not found or unreachable". [→](/guides/organisation-secrets#2-reference-it-where-the-value-belongs)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: pipeline diagnosis" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: pipeline diagnosis" }}>
**Full CI cluster** - a step that stopped because no node had room no longer tells you to enable autoscaling you already have. It says no node had room, and to add nodes, raise the node group's maximum, or turn autoscaling on if it is off. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#when-something-fails)

</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week" }}>
**Drafts** - a draft staged from a file on a deployed Stack keeps an add-on's dependency on a parent the file leaves unchanged. Deploying it used to drop the dependency. [→](/guides/create-and-change-a-stack#with-the-cli)

**Agent release** - the page of the cluster's own agent release shows a link to **Agent settings** in place of the three Helm buttons, which Ankra refuses there. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights#cluster-and-helm)

**Queued runs** - a run waiting on your organisation's **Runs at the same time** limit says how many of its runs start before it, or that it is next, on the run page and in `ankra pipeline get`. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#why-a-run-is-waiting)

**Scanning** - the offer to turn on scanning is no longer counted as a fix, so a new organisation sees no security count for it. [→](/guides/cluster-security#turn-on-scanning)

**Pipeline steps** - a step whose `runs_on.arch` no node in the cluster has fails naming that architecture and the ones the nodes have. An image with no build for the nodes' platform fails as the step's own failure, without a retry. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#when-something-fails)

**Refused reads** - a page with one refused part gives the same result every time. By chance it used to read **Your access or organisation changed.** or stay open. [→](/guides/why-was-i-refused#your-role)

</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Security" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Security" }}>
**Security fixes** - a fix plan is no longer refused as "no longer offered" because one read of your clusters missed it. Ankra reads once more first. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: clusters" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: clusters" }}>
**Same-named members** - when a Stack Ankra writes on a cluster it built, such as the networking stack, names an add-on one of your Stacks already holds, Ankra now leaves yours unchanged. Its first publish used to lay its own chart and values over yours. [→](/concepts/stacks#a-member-belongs-to-one-stack)

**Pod metrics** - on a cluster with no metrics source, a pod's **Metrics** tab says so once and offers **Connect a metrics source**, with a link to the pod's namespace in Ankra's built-in metrics. [→](/integrations/prometheus)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Kubernetes lists" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Kubernetes lists" }}>
**Kubernetes lists** - only a row's checkbox selects it. Clicking a name or another cell on a resource or pod list used to tick that row too. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights#resource-lists)

**Deleting resources** - an object a controller created, such as a CronJob's Job or a pod of a workload a Stack deploys, can be deleted. The review names the controller and what it does next: a CronJob keeps its schedule. Both used to be refused as declared by the Stack. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights#change-a-resource)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: a new application's first pull request" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: a new application's first pull request" }}>
**Dependabot file** - the `.github/dependabot.yml` Ankra adds now carries a seven-day cooldown, so a new release is offered once it has been public for a week. The setup pull request's own scan no longer reports a finding against that file. [→](/pipelines/generated-pipelines#dependency-updates-with-dependabot)

**Proof check** - with no staging cluster chosen, the **Ankra proof** check reads **Off: no staging cluster chosen** and posts no comment. It used to read **Not proven** above seven steps that never ran. [→](/pipelines/generated-pipelines#the-setup-pull-request)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Deploy provenance shows the last check of each environment" }}>
## Deploy provenance shows the last check of each environment

The **Deploy provenance** card under an application's **Settings → Registry** opens on the last check of each environment: when it ran, who asked for it, the version it judged, the result, and each image with its digest. Ankra keeps the check it makes at every release. **Check now** checks every environment and deploys nothing.

[Deploy provenance →](/security/deploy-provenance#set-the-mode)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: runs the build cluster could not run" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: runs the build cluster could not run" }}>
**Checks that could not run** - when the build cluster has no room for a setup pull request's checks, Ankra AI no longer commits a fix to the pull request. Nothing in it was tested, so the **Ankra PR shepherd** check reads **Needs a person** and says the run could not test it. [→](/concepts/applications#the-pr-shepherd)

**Merge train** - a car whose run the CI cluster could not run is rebuilt, and after three such runs it leaves the train as untested. A car stopped that way used to leave on its first such run, as if the pull request had failed. [→](/pipelines/merge-train#several-cars-at-once)

</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["CLI", "Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "ankra cluster connect registers a cluster you already run and prints its install command" }}>
## `ankra cluster connect` registers a cluster you already run and prints its install command

`ankra cluster connect <name>` does from the terminal what the portal's **Connect a cluster** page does: it registers the cluster and prints the Helm command that installs the Ankra agent. Before, the CLI needed an ImportCluster file for this. A name another cluster in the organisation already has is refused and nothing is changed, from the CLI and from the portal. Needs CLI 0.79.0.

[Connect a cluster from the CLI →](/guides/import-cluster#from-the-cli)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Pipelines run on a cluster with no StorageClass" }}>
## Pipelines run on a cluster with no StorageClass

A run's workspace needs a volume, and a cluster you connected with no storage driver had nothing to create one, so every run failed. Ankra now keeps each run's workspace on the disk of its node on such a cluster. The agent sets it up itself, on Kubernetes 1.30 or later, and keeps 15% of the disk free. A StorageClass you name, or a default one that creates volumes, is still used first.

[Workspace storage →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#workspace-storage)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: build fixes by Ankra AI" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: build fixes by Ankra AI" }}>
**Build fix time limit** - a build fix by Ankra AI ends at its 45 minute limit, also while it waits for a check that never reports. It used to stay open until that wait ran out, and the application's history read as still fixing for up to an hour. [→](/concepts/applications#when-the-setup-pull-request-does-not-pass-its-checks)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-18" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: the deploy form" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: the deploy form" }}>
**Public hostname** - a name you type directly under the cluster's own zone is now said to be published by the cluster, which it always was. The form used to say Ankra does not publish a record for it. [→](/guides/use-your-own-domain)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: application History, Logs and Cost" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: application History, Logs and Cost" }}>
**History** - **Compare versions** puts two versions side by side, with the image each runs and where, and promotes one from there. [→](/platform/application-page#history)

**History** - **Export** downloads the changes shown as CSV or JSON, and an open change steps to the one before or after it. [→](/platform/application-page#history)

**Logs** - opens on a search of the last 24 hours when the cluster keeps the lines, and on **Live** when it does not. [→](/platform/application-page#logs)

**Logs** - drag across the bars to narrow the time, or use **Zoom to the most errors**. **The same time in Metrics** opens Metrics on those minutes. [→](/platform/application-page#logs)

**Logs** - **Export** saves the lines as a text file or as NDJSON, and **Load older lines** reads further back. [→](/platform/application-page#logs)

**Cost** - a warning names each environment over its monthly budget, and **Against the budget** puts each environment's monthly rate beside its budget. [→](/platform/application-page#cost)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Each cluster sends Ankra built-in metrics, with nothing to install or connect" }}>
## Each cluster sends Ankra built-in metrics, with nothing to install or connect

Every 30 seconds, the Ankra agent sends Ankra a fixed set of node, namespace and workload metrics, and request rates and response times per namespace from Traefik. Ankra keeps them for 30 days. They draw an application's **Metrics** view and a cluster's **Metrics** page, and metric alerts and Ankra AI read them. They are on by default, and nothing is charged for them during the pilot. For your applications' own metrics, connect your own Prometheus.

[Cluster metrics →](/platform/cluster-metrics)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Kubernetes lists: select rows, shift-click a range, act on the selection" }}>
## Kubernetes lists: select rows, shift-click a range, act on the selection

Resource, pod and node lists have a checkbox on each row. Shift-click selects every row between two, and the header checkbox selects every row shown. A bar above the list says how many rows are selected and deletes, restarts or cordons them, after the same live review as a single row.

[Kubernetes resource lists →](/platform/kubernetes-insights#resource-lists)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: deploys and the audit log" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: deploys and the audit log" }}>
**Applications** - a deploy checks CPU and memory before it starts. A value Kubernetes cannot read, or a request above its limit, is refused with the reason, and nothing changes. [→](/reference/application-descriptor#default-options)

**Applications** - a deploy to a parked environment keeps it parked. A copy count sent with it is what waking brings back. [→](/concepts/applications#remove-a-deployment-from-one-cluster)

**Audit log** - a deploy that changes an environment's deploy options is recorded in the audit log. [→](/guides/audit-log#what-is-recorded)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Security" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Security" }}>
**Fix page** - **All *n* clusters at once** is disabled, with the reason beside it, while a cluster that already runs the new release does not report it healthy. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)

**Fix page** - a staged step held by an unhealthy release links to the add-on's page on that cluster. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)

**k3s images** - an issue Ankra can confirm is in an image k3s itself ships is listed as **Came with the cluster**, apart from the changes that are yours to make. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week" }}>
**Offline clusters** - a staged security add-on upgrade leaves out a cluster whose agent is not connected and names it, so no later step waits for it. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)

**Deleted Stacks** - a Stack with nothing left on the cluster finishes deleting while the cluster is stopped or offline, and a stopped cluster raises no stalled notice for work it could not run. [→](/concepts/stacks#uninstall-or-delete)

**Application Security** - an application's **Security** view reads top to bottom: **What needs you**, **Fix first**, **What stops a release** and **What is running**, then every finding. [→](/concepts/applications#security-scanning)

**Remote commands** - delayed watchdog startup no longer resets a command's timeout or overlooks a disconnected SSH session. [→](/platform/operations#remote-command-timeouts)

**GitOps** - equivalent imported defaults and file references no longer reopen preserved-file decisions. Real content and policy changes still need a decision. [→](/concepts/gitops#diverged-files)

**Emails** - every email from Ankra has a cleaner layout that follows dark mode where your mail app supports it, with an amber or red line across the top of a warning or a critical one. [→](/platform/user-profile#notifications)

**Support** - a ticket the Ankra team has already answered no longer receives the automatic message saying it was escalated to an engineer. [→](/platform/support#ticket-lifecycle)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["GitOps", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: GitOps and applications" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: GitOps and applications" }}>
**GitOps** - a Stack you remove while Git and the platform both have changes stays removed. It used to come back empty. [→](/concepts/gitops#when-git-and-the-platform-both-change)

**GitOps** - a stopped or offline cluster's GitOps status reads paused, with the reason, instead of an old failure. [→](/concepts/gitops#sync-status)

**Applications** - removing a deployment while the cluster's Git repository has newer changes now finishes instead of reading as failed. The commit to Git follows at the next sync. [→](/concepts/applications#remove-a-deployment-from-one-cluster)

**Applications** - deployments on a deleted cluster are removed from the application, so a new build no longer tries to deploy to them. [→](/concepts/applications#remove-a-deployment-from-one-cluster)

**Applications** - when a deployment has failed in one environment and another still serves the application, the Overview says where it serves. A stopped cluster reads as not connected. [→](/concepts/applications#the-application-workspace)

**Applications** - a repository that is not a service, such as a tools image or a batch job, no longer shows a failed setup: its page says what builds it. [→](/concepts/applications#a-repository-that-is-not-a-service)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: AI" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: AI" }}>
**Stop all AI** - **Stop all AI** also stops AI pull request reviews and the AI step of a new application's setup. A skipped review says why on its **Ankra AI review** check, and the setup uses Ankra's templates. [→](/platform/ai-autonomy#stop-ankra-ai)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: Helm releases and builds" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: Helm releases and builds" }}>
**Helm releases** - when Ankra refuses a Helm rollback or uninstall, the dialog shows the reason under **Rollback was refused** or **Uninstall was refused** instead of saying the result could not be confirmed. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights#cluster-and-helm)

**Builds on Ankra** - a build stage that lists one of BuildKit's own platform arguments, such as `TARGETOS`, in `build_args` now builds on Ankra platform builders. It was refused as `build_fallback_unsupported`. A pipeline Ankra generates no longer lists them. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#ankra-platform-builders)

**Helm uninstall** - `ankra cluster helm uninstall` and the API now refuse a release an Ankra add-on manages, as upgrade and rollback already did. Uninstall the add-on, or redeploy its Stack to clear a stuck release. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights#cluster-and-helm)

**Helm release API** - a release's detail carries `metadata.is_cluster_agent`, true for the cluster's own agent release, which Ankra refuses to upgrade, roll back or uninstall. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights#cluster-and-helm)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: clusters" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: clusters" }}>
**Cluster agent** - a change saved while a cluster's agent is offline waits for the agent and says so, instead of starting an operation nothing can run. Retry and redeploy say why they cannot run. [→](/concepts/cluster-agent#changes-made-while-the-agent-is-offline)

**Connecting a cluster** - **Connect an existing cluster** shows the install command as soon as you name the cluster. Under it, a picture of your cluster and Ankra says **Connecting**, then **Connected** when the agent reports. [→](/guides/import-cluster#connect-an-existing-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-17" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week: backups and clusters" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: backups and clusters" }}>
**Backups** - a restore point taken into a backup vault on UpCloud Object Storage completes. Before, UpCloud refused the first upload and no restore point was sealed. [→](/guides/backups#let-ankra-create-the-bucket)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-16" tags={["Clusters", "API"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra Cloud has a status page at ankra.cloud/status, with each service in every public zone" }}>
## Ankra Cloud has a status page at ankra.cloud/status, with each service in every public zone

ankra.cloud/status shows how Ankra Cloud is doing: API and consoles, DNS, and in each public zone servers, block storage, networking, load balancers, Managed PostgreSQL, Managed Kubernetes and object storage. It lists open incidents and maintenance in progress with every update, scheduled maintenance and the last 30 days, with times in UTC. Ankra staff publish it. Scripts can read the same report without a credential at `https://cloud.ankra.app/v1/status`.

[Ankra Cloud status →](/platform/support#ankra-cloud-status)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-16" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "An application's menu follows the work: Overview, a Deploy group, then one row per view" }}>
## An application's menu follows the work: Overview, a Deploy group, then one row per view

An application's menu now starts with **Overview** and a **Deploy** group that holds **Environments**, **Pipelines** and **Previews**. Then come **History**, **Logs**, **Metrics**, **Security**, **Data**, **Cost**, **Secrets**, **Architecture** and **Settings**. **Health** became **Logs** and **Metrics**, **Protect** became **Security** and **Data**, and **Ship** became **Environments**, with **Previews** as a page of its own. Links saved before the change still open where they meant.

[The application page →](/platform/application-page)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-16" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped this week" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week" }}>
**Staged upgrades** - the platform holds the order of a staged security add-on upgrade, so a later step is refused from any client until the earlier clusters report healthy. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)

**Home** - under its question, Home lists what needs you with one button each, then what runs in your organisation. [→](/platform/home)

**Agent release** - Ankra refuses a Helm upgrade, rollback or uninstall of a cluster's own agent release, `ankra-agent`, from the page, the CLI and the API, and for an upgrade or a rollback says where the agent is changed instead. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights#cluster-and-helm)

**Helm releases** - `ankra cluster helm get` reports a release's chart, chart version and app version, and an upgrade with no `--version` keeps the chart version the release runs. [→](/reference/cli/cluster#ankra-cluster-helm-upgrade)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-16" tags={["AI", "Clusters"]} description="Also shipped this week: AI, builds and clusters" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: AI, builds and clusters" }}>
**Stop all AI** - **Stop all AI** also stops the analysis written when an alert fires and the scheduled health check. Both still called a model before. [→](/platform/ai-autonomy#stop-ankra-ai)

**Builds Ankra starts** - with **Build on Ankra when your cluster cannot** off, Ankra no longer builds your code by itself either, such as a new application's setup commit or a preview with no image. [→](/pipelines/how-it-works#the-big-picture)

**AI fix approvers** - **Who may approve** picks from your organisation's admins. The field asked for Slack member IDs, which matched nobody and blocked every approval. [→](/platform/ai-autonomy#fixing-alerts-automatically)

**Node controls** - the portal offers deleting a node group and changing a managed pool's node count only to roles the platform accepts them from. [→](/platform/cluster-nodes#before-you-start)

**Stack removal** - the Stack editor says what removing a member or deleting a Stack deletes, volume claims included. It used to say persistent data stays. [→](/concepts/stacks#uninstalling-a-stack)

**Agent chart** - `metrics_collect.enabled: false` and `k8s_watch.strip_fields: false` take effect from agent 2.1.1339. Earlier agents ignored an explicit `false`. [→](/reference/agent-helm-values#metrics)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-15" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "A new organisation starts on one question, with four ways in" }}>
## A new organisation starts on one question, with four ways in

After you sign up, Ankra opens on **Where do you want to start?** with four choices: **Connect my AI agent**, **Try a playground**, **Put my app online** and **Connect my own cluster**. Each has its own page, and the ones you skip wait on Home under **Starting points**. **Put my app online** takes a GitHub repository to a live HTTPS address on a free playground and builds it again on every push.

[Choose where to start →](/get-started/start-here)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-15" tags={["Clusters", "Security"]} rss={{ title: "Each cluster has an OS updates mode: automatic or advisory, per cluster and per node group" }}>
## Each cluster has an OS updates mode: automatic or advisory, per cluster and per node group

A cluster Ankra built on a cloud provider now has an OS updates mode. In `automatic`, Ankra starts the rolling patch run in the maintenance window, one drained node at a time. In `advisory`, nothing patches a host until a person starts a run. A node group can override its cluster. With no mode chosen, a cluster a person confirmed is not production becomes automatic 14 days after a notification names the date. Every other cluster, production included, stays advisory.

[When nobody chose a mode →](/guides/os-patching#when-nobody-chose-a-mode)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-15" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Security reports vulnerable operating system packages on your nodes" }}>
## Security reports vulnerable operating system packages on your nodes

The Security Center now matches the operating system packages on each Ubuntu or Debian node, such as OpenSSH and OpenSSL, against the distribution's security advisories. In **Security → Vulnerabilities**, choose **Node package vulnerabilities** under **Show** to list them. An advisory is reported when CISA lists it as known exploited, or when it is critical or high with a published fix. It covers clusters Ankra built on a cloud provider, from a daily dry run that changes nothing on the nodes.

[Node package findings →](/guides/cluster-security#node-package-findings)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-15" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "A Kubernetes upgrade moves a CloudNativePG primary to a replica before it drains the node" }}>
## A Kubernetes upgrade moves a CloudNativePG primary to a replica before it drains the node

During a Kubernetes version upgrade of a k3s or kubeadm cluster Ankra built, a node that carries a CloudNativePG primary no longer parks on the database's disruption budget. Ankra switches the primary over to a healthy replica on another node, waits for it, then drains. When it cannot, it puts the node back in service and the upgrade stops there, naming the database. A single-instance database is refused until its owner sets an annotation that accepts the restart.

[CloudNativePG databases →](/guides/kubernetes-upgrades#cloudnativepg-databases)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-15" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Each cluster has its own Security page, with the fix plans that touch it" }}>
## Each cluster has its own Security page, with the fix plans that touch it

A cluster's menu now has **Security**. The page opens on a one-line verdict and four numbers: **Issues found**, **Known to be used by attackers**, **Fixes ready** and **Last scan**. **Needs you** lists every fix plan that touches the cluster, each with **Review fix**, and **Where the issues are** breaks the issues down by stack. A cluster's issues are counted once, so the overview and the cluster's own page agree.

[A cluster's Security page →](/guides/cluster-security#a-cluster’s-security-page)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-15" tags={["Pipelines", "AI"]} description="Also shipped this week: pipelines and AI" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: pipelines and AI" }}>
**Stage workspace size** - in a pipeline with per-step workspaces, a stage can set its own `workspace.size`, so one large step no longer sizes every step. [→](/guides/pipeline-reference#fields-common-to-every-stage)

**Run page** - a run of a repository connected without an application opens on its own portal page, with steps, logs, tests and findings. [→](/guides/ankra-pipelines#connect-a-repository-push-see-the-run)

**Previews over http** - the pull request's preview card says when the staging cluster serves a preview over plain http, and what to add. [→](/guides/pr-preview-environments#troubleshooting)

**Your app online** - choosing a repository on the first screen also turns on AI code review, pull request previews and database backups. [→](/get-started/put-your-app-online#what-the-setup-turns-on)

**AI review limits** - a pull request gets 3 automatic reviews by default, down from 5, and one that changes only docs, lockfiles or a bot's dependency bump is skipped. [→](/platform/ai-code-review#the-two-budgets)

**AI turns** - no AI turn runs past 40 rounds of tool calls, whatever an agent's `max_tool_calls` says. [→](/platform/ai-agents#budget)

**Conditions** - the Tickets board's **Conditions** button opens recurring problems in the portal, each with its tickets. [→](/platform/ai-board#conditions)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-15" tags={["Clusters", "Security"]} description="Also shipped this week: clusters and security" rss={{ title: "Also shipped this week: clusters and security" }}>
**Setup stopped** - `ankra cluster list` marks a cluster whose create failed as `creating (setup stopped)`, and `ankra cluster info` prints where it stopped. [→](/guides/cluster-will-not-come-up#find-where-setup-stopped)

**Kubernetes lists** - the name search on most lists covers the whole list, resources have a read-only **YAML** tab, and a pod page has **Delete pod**. [→](/platform/kubernetes-insights)

**Node drain** - the drain page asks for a separate tick for each risk its review names, such as local data. [→](/platform/kubernetes-nodes#drain-a-node)

**Stuck add-ons** - a Stack whose add-on removal is stuck offers **Force remove**. [→](/concepts/stacks#force-removal-of-stuck-add-ons)

**Hetzner limits** - **Review cluster** warns when the Hetzner project was refused for a limit in the last day. [→](/guides/hetzner-clusters)

**Playground card** - **Add a cluster** says whether a new playground is a free trial or billed to your Ankra Cloud account. [→](/guides/cluster-sandbox)

**Fix activity** - a pull request that fixes several of an application's issues at once is one row in **Security → Fix activity**. [→](/guides/cluster-security#how-fixes-work)

**Fix attempts** - a Security Center fix stops for a person after two attempts, not three. [→](/guides/fix-a-vulnerable-image)

**Cluster credentials** - **Settings → Credentials** lists the credentials a cluster uses again. Since 23 October it said the cluster was not found. [→](/platform/credentials#see-what-a-cluster-uses)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-14" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "See and run the merge train from an application's Pipelines view" }}>
## See and run the merge train from an application's Pipelines view

**Applications → your application → Pipelines** now has a **Merge train** section. It shows whether the train is on, the **Queue** in merge order with each pull request's status, attempt and test run, and what recently left the train. From there you can switch the train on, add a pull request, move one to the front, remove one and change the settings, each confirmed in a dialog. Before, only `ankra pipeline train` and pull request comments showed it.

[The merge train →](/pipelines/merge-train)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-14" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "One page lists every AI pull request review and what the authors did about it" }}>
## One page lists every AI pull request review and what the authors did about it

**Ankra AI → Code reviews** lists every pull request Ankra AI reviewed, newest first, with its verdict and findings. Filter by repository, period and verdict, and read the summary: pull requests reviewed, findings raised, the share acted on and repositories covered. Open a review to see what Ankra AI said and what became of each finding, for example **Acted on** or **Left as is**. An API token reads the same under `/api/v1/org/ai-gateway/code-reviews`.

[Read what the reviews found →](/platform/ai-code-review#read-what-the-reviews-found)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-14" tags={["API"]} rss={{ title: "API tokens reach organisation, billing, notification, audit and credential routes" }}>
## API tokens reach organisation, billing, notification, audit and credential routes

Many routes that only the portal could call now answer an API token under `/api/v1`: organisation settings and invitations, billing and invoices, the notification inbox and mutes, audit logs, credential checks and more cluster reads. Each checks the permission the portal's route checks. Some refuse a token pinned to a project, and a read with no `/api/v1` route of its own is served only to a personal token with no scopes and no project pin.

[API introduction →](/api-reference/introduction)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-14" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "The Slack app asks for fewer permissions and always shows a way forward" }}>
## The Slack app asks for fewer permissions and always shows a way forward

A new install of the Ankra Slack app no longer asks for email addresses or direct message details, which it never used. An install started from Slack ends on a page with **Continue in Ankra** instead of a dead end, and `/ankra` in a workspace no organisation holds links to the same place. Anything after `/ankra` that is not a command shows the help, as does sending the bot **help**. Connected workspaces need no reinstall.

[What the Slack app can see →](/platform/ai-connections#what-the-slack-app-can-see)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-14" tags={["Pipelines", "Security"]} rss={{ title: "The pipeline's Trivy scan ignores Trivy settings committed to the repository" }}>
## The pipeline's Trivy scan ignores Trivy settings committed to the repository

A `.trivyignore`, `trivy.yaml` or `trivy-secret.yaml` in the checkout, and `TRIVY_*` variables in a stage's env, no longer change what the scan step reports. The gate now judges what the platform scanned, so a pull request cannot drop a finding before it is judged. Semgrep's `.semgrepignore` and `nosemgrep` still apply, and code finding dispositions remain the audited way to waive a Semgrep or Checkov finding.

[The scan takes its settings from the platform →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#the-scan-takes-its-settings-from-the-platform)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-14" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Rename a cluster in place, without rebuilding it" }}>
## Rename a cluster in place, without rebuilding it

`ankra cluster rename <cluster> <new-name>` gives a running cluster a new name in Ankra. Nothing is rebuilt and the cluster id does not change, so kubeconfig contexts, tokens, metrics, logs and backups keep working. Node names, the DNS zone and the GitOps directory keep the old name. An ImportCluster file that still names the old cluster is refused by `ankra cluster apply`, never applied as a second cluster.

[Rename a Cluster →](/guides/rename-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-13" tags={["Pipelines", "Security"]} rss={{ title: "A new application's setup pull request adds a Dependabot configuration" }}>
## A new application's setup pull request adds a Dependabot configuration

When you create an application from a GitHub repository, Ankra's setup pull request now also adds `.github/dependabot.yml`. Dependabot then opens weekly update pull requests for the ecosystems the repository uses: minor and patch updates grouped, major ones one by one, and at most five open per entry. Each runs the Ankra pipeline before you merge it. A repository that already configures Dependabot or Renovate keeps its own.

[Dependency updates with Dependabot →](/pipelines/generated-pipelines#dependency-updates-with-dependabot)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-13" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra's managed AI access runs on Claude Haiku 5.5" }}>
## Ankra's managed AI access runs on Claude Haiku 5.5

An organisation on Ankra's managed AI access, with no Anthropic or OpenRouter key of its own, now gets Claude Haiku 5.5 on the Expert, Think and Quick tiers, and so on every AI feature that follows a tier: code review, deploy analysis, troubleshooting and CI/CD generation. An organisation with its own OpenRouter or Anthropic key keeps the models it had, and so does one that has edited **Chat models**.

[Default models →](/platform/ai-provider#default-models)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-13" tags={["AI", "API"]} rss={{ title: "28 more tools for MCP clients, from audit logs to cost reports" }}>
## 28 more tools for MCP clients, from audit logs to cost reports

MCP clients can call 28 more tools. The reads include `list_organisation_audit_logs`, `get_cost_report`, `get_pipeline_report`, `get_security_remediations`, `get_cluster_security_posture` and `get_application_backups`. The writes include `set_application_database_backup`, `rename_application_environment` and `manage_test_quarantine`. Each tool checks the permission its API route checks. The portal's own chat does not offer them.

[MCP tool reference →](/platform/mcp-tools)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-13" tags={["Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "The classic portal is retired, and its old addresses open the same page in the portal" }}>
## The classic portal is retired, and its old addresses open the same page in the portal

Classic Ankra is gone. An old classic address in an email, a pull request comment, a Slack or Teams card or a bookmark now opens its page in the portal under `/next`, and an address with no counterpart opens Home. The second sign-in step, the CLI sign-in confirmation and the returns from Bitbucket and Stripe are portal pages too. A page you may not open says why in place. Tasks with no portal page each have a CLI command.

[Where did it go? →](/platform/classic-to-portal)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-13" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "Welcome credit now lasts 30 days, and the Startups programme grants €1,000 for six months" }}>
## Welcome credit now lasts 30 days, and the Startups programme grants €1,000 for six months

The €59 welcome credit a new organisation unlocks by saving a payment method is now valid for 30 days instead of 60. The sign-up credit for new Ankra Cloud accounts changes the same way. An approved Startups programme application now adds €1,000 of credit for six months, down from €25,000 for twelve months. Credit granted before this change keeps its original amount and expiry date.

[Welcome credit →](/platform/billing#welcome-credit)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-13" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "AI assistance beside support tickets" }}>
## AI assistance beside support tickets

Staff can preview an AI rewrite of a reply and choose whether to apply it. A sidebar assistant uses the selected ticket, recent messages and Cloud and Platform docs to suggest investigation steps. Private notes are included only when selected, and findings can be copied into a private note draft. Nothing is sent automatically. Live product admin diagnostics remain separate.

[Use staff AI assistance →](/platform/support#staff-ai-assistance)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-12" tags={["Fixes", "AI"]} rss={{ title: "Support continues into AI chat automatically" }}>
## Support continues into AI chat automatically

Support starts the Cloud or Platform sign-in handoff when its session is missing or expires. It remembers the product previously used for Support, with Cloud as the default for a direct first visit. An unsent AI question is restored for review after reconnecting; signing out keeps that tab signed out. If a handoff fails, retry links remain available without a redirect loop. Successful ticket submissions now use a neutral confirmation with a green accent; red is reserved for errors.

[Open Support →](/platform/support#ankra-support-the-ai-chat)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-12" tags={["Security", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Accept a vulnerability for a whole namespace, and keep it accepted across deploys" }}>
## Accept a vulnerability for a whole namespace, and keep it accepted across deploys

A disposition can now cover a CVE in every workload of one namespace on one cluster. It keeps holding after rollouts and image rebuilds, so it suits a transitive dependency's CVE in your own application. Workload dispositions on a Deployment now survive rollouts too. Pick the scope in the **Accept this risk** dialog, with `ankra security dispositions create --scope namespace`, or in Terraform.

[What a disposition is pinned to →](/guides/cluster-security#acknowledge-or-accept-the-risk)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-11" tags={["CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Create your Ankra account from the terminal" }}>
## Create your Ankra account from the terminal

`curl -fsSL https://ankra.ai/install | sh` installs the CLI and runs `ankra onboard`, which now creates an account as well as signing in to one. Open the link it prints, sign up with GitHub, Google or email, check that the code on the page matches the code in your terminal, and approve. Onboarding then carries on in the terminal: an organisation, a free playground and skills for your coding agents. Needs CLI v0.75.0 or later.

[Sign up or sign in with ankra onboard →](/integrations/ankra-cli#sign-up-or-sign-in-with-ankra-onboard)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-09" tags={["Pipelines", "Cost"]} rss={{ title: "A run too big for your CI cluster moves to a CI playground on Ankra Cloud" }}>
## A run too big for your CI cluster moves to a CI playground on Ankra Cloud

When no node of your CI cluster can hold a run's largest step, Ankra now moves the whole run to a CI playground: an Ankra Cloud cluster with pipeline workers. A run that fits the trial plan uses your organisation's free trial playground. Otherwise, when the organisation's Ankra Cloud account can pay, it gets a billed playground at the smallest plan that fits, removed 5 minutes after its last run. Turn it off with `ankra org ci-settings set --auto-playground=false`.

[A CI playground →](/pipelines/where-pipelines-run#a-ci-playground)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-09" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "GPU chat runs without a GPU, and can serve its chat UI to your organisation through a tunnel" }}>
## GPU chat runs without a GPU, and can serve its chat UI to your organisation through a tunnel

Package `gpu-chat` 1.1.0 adds model size `0`: a small Qwen3 model on llama.cpp that runs on ordinary nodes, behind the same OpenAI-compatible endpoint. Setup refuses it where no node has room for the model server. With `chat_ui_access` set to 1, an Ankra Cloud tunnel can serve the Open WebUI chat, and behind a tunnel zone whose access policy is the organisation only your members are let in. Nothing is published on the cluster's ingress.

[GPU chat →](/reference/managed-services#gpu-chat)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-09" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Uninstall a Stack and install it again, keeping it in Ankra and in Git" }}>
## Uninstall a Stack and install it again, keeping it in Ankra and in Git

**Uninstall stack** takes every add-on, manifest and application in a Stack off the cluster while the Stack stays declared in Ankra and in your GitOps repository. **Install stack** deploys it again with what it holds now, including changes saved while it was off. Both are under **More** on the Stack page of the new portal, and in the CLI as `ankra cluster stacks uninstall` and `install`. Back the Stack up first: data goes as it does on a delete.

[Uninstalling a Stack →](/concepts/stacks#uninstalling-a-stack)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-09" tags={["Fixes"]} description="Also shipped: new how-to guides" rss={{ title: "Also shipped: new how-to guides" }}>
**Projects** - a step-by-step guide to creating a project, moving resources, project-only members and pinned tokens. [→](/guides/set-up-projects)

**Organisation secrets** - store a value with `ankra secrets`, reference its sentinel and rotate it in place. [→](/guides/organisation-secrets)

**Object storage buckets** - create, follow and delete buckets with `ankra bucket`. [→](/guides/object-storage-buckets)

**Cost** - budgets, autopilot tiers, approving decisions and reading the ledger, in order. [→](/guides/cost-budgets-and-autopilot)

**Domains** - one guide to delegating a domain, serving a Cloudflare or webhook zone, and publishing records. [→](/guides/use-your-own-domain)

**Ankra Cloud** - choosing plans, CPU pools, root disks and checking quota before you build. [→](/guides/ankra-cloud-servers-and-disks)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-08" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Patch the operating system of a cluster's nodes one host at a time from the CLI and the API" }}>
## Patch the operating system of a cluster's nodes one host at a time from the CLI and the API

Rolling OS patching now has REST routes and `ankra cluster <provider> os-patches` commands beside its chat and MCP tools: read a cluster's patch status, dry-run the pending security updates, start a run that drains and patches one host at a time, and set the maintenance window and policy. It serves clusters Ankra built on Ankra Cloud, AWS, DigitalOcean, Hetzner, OVHcloud, Scaleway and UpCloud. Needs CLI v0.73.0.

[OS Security Patching →](/guides/os-patching)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-08" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Manage projects and GitHub OIDC trust policies from Terraform" }}>
## Manage projects and GitHub OIDC trust policies from Terraform

Provider 0.5.0 adds `ankra_project` and `ankra_oidc_trust_policy`, and data sources for projects, trust policies and the organisation's Kubernetes access policy. Create a team's project and pin its CI token to it in one plan, and let a GitHub workflow sign in with no stored Ankra secret. The access policy stays read-only: a change to it still needs an owner or admin.

[Projects and GitHub OIDC trust →](/integrations/terraform#projects-and-github-oidc-trust)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-08" tags={["Security", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Read and export the audit trail from the terminal" }}>
## Read and export the audit trail from the terminal

`ankra org audit-logs list` shows the newest entries, filtered by window, actor type, outcome, cluster, action and more. `ankra org audit-logs export` downloads the trail as CSV or JSON, and `ankra cluster audit-logs` narrows the list to one cluster. All three need `audit.read` and work with a service token, so a scheduled job can forward the trail to a SIEM. Needs CLI v0.73.0.

[Exporting from the CLI →](/security/audit-export#exporting-from-the-cli)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-07" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Remove one chosen node from a node group" }}>
## Remove one chosen node from a node group

`ankra cluster node-group remove-node <cluster> <group> <node>` retires the node you name, such as one on a failing host, and lowers the group's count by one so nothing recreates it. The node is drained through its PodDisruptionBudgets before its server is deleted; a refused drain keeps it in service unless you add `--force-drain`. Needs CLI v0.72.0.

[ankra cluster node-group remove-node →](/reference/cli/cluster#ankra-cluster-node-group-remove-node)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-07" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Promote a build between environments and set pull request previews from the CLI" }}>
## Promote a build between environments and set pull request previews from the CLI

`ankra application promote <app> --from staging --to production` moves the build one environment runs onto the next without building again, after showing what would change. `ankra application previews <app>` shows whether pull requests get a preview, and `previews enable`, `disable` and `follow` set the repository's rule. Needs CLI v0.71.0.

[ankra application promote →](/reference/cli/application#ankra-application-promote)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-07" tags={["AI", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Connect Slack from the terminal" }}>
## Connect Slack from the terminal

`ankra slack connect` prints the Slack install address and waits until someone approves the install in a browser, on any machine. `--no-wait` prints the address and exits, and `ankra slack status` lists the connected workspaces. It needs an organisation administrator. Needs CLI v0.71.0.

[ankra slack →](/reference/cli/slack)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-07" tags={["AI", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Connect Claude Code, Cursor or Codex to the Ankra MCP server with one command" }}>
## Connect Claude Code, Cursor or Codex to the Ankra MCP server with one command

`ankra mcp connect --client claude-code|cursor|codex` creates a read-only MCP token (`--write` adds the mutating tools) that expires in 90 days, stores it in a file only you can read and registers the server with the client. The token is never printed or written into the client's config. Run it again to renew. Needs CLI v0.71.0.

[Connect with the Ankra CLI →](/platform/mcp-server#connect-with-the-ankra-cli)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-07" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "Bitbucket Cloud pull request reviews run on your own AI keys" }}>
## Bitbucket Cloud pull request reviews run on your own AI keys

The reviews you ask for by mentioning Ankra in a Bitbucket Cloud pull request comment, and the automatic reviews of pull requests you open, now run on the AI keys on your profile, as they already did on GitHub. Ankra matches you only through the Bitbucket account you connected under **Profile** → **Connected accounts**. If you connected Bitbucket before this change, connect it again once. GitLab reviews still run on your organisation's AI.

[Your Own AI Keys →](/platform/personal-ai-keys#reviews-of-your-pull-requests)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-07" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "GPU chat: an OpenAI-compatible model endpoint as a managed service" }}>
## GPU chat: an OpenAI-compatible model endpoint as a managed service

The managed-service catalogue has a new package, `gpu-chat`, with the capability **Inference**. It runs an open model with vLLM on one of your GPU nodes and gives connected applications `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `OPENAI_MODEL`, so any OpenAI client works against it inside the cluster. Choose the model size, from Qwen3 8B on an L4 up to Qwen3.8 27B on Blackwell, and the replica count. Setup needs a node with NVIDIA GPUs.

[OpenAI-compatible inference →](/guides/connect-to-a-managed-service#openai-compatible-inference)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-07" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "See, consent to and end every Ankra support session in your organisation" }}>
## See, consent to and end every Ankra support session in your organisation

Ankra support now helps with a ticket through a support session: one hour at most, acting as one member, with a recorded reason. Sessions are read-only unless an admin gives a single-use consent code. You get a security notification when one opens, every member sees them under **Ankra support access**, admins can end one at once, and everything done is in your audit log, as are Ankra staff views outside a session.

[Ankra Support Access →](/security/support-access)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-06" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "Run your own AI chats and pull request reviews on your own API keys" }}>
## Run your own AI chats and pull request reviews on your own API keys

Add one or more Anthropic or OpenRouter API keys to your profile, in the order you want them tried. Your AI chats and the reviews of your GitHub pull requests then run on your own account, including the monthly API credits some Claude plans come with, and never draw on your organisation's allowance. A key that runs out is skipped; nothing moves to another account mid-conversation.

[Your Own AI Keys →](/platform/personal-ai-keys)
</Update>
