<Update label="2026-11-06" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "One page for the whole flow from a repository to production, on the console, the CLI and Claude Code" }}>
## One page for the whole flow from a repository to production, on the console, the CLI and Claude Code

A new page and film follow one repository from setup pull request to production on each surface, list what Ankra refuses and why, and say plainly that pull request previews have an open bug. A matrix shows each step's exact page, command or tool.

[From prompt and code to production →](/use-cases/from-prompt-and-code-to-production)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-05" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Turn the Gateway API on for a cluster that already exists" }}>
## Turn the Gateway API on for a cluster that already exists

A cluster Ankra built with the networking stack can now get the Gateway API option after it is created: the Traefik it already runs then serves `Gateway` and `HTTPRoute` resources beside Ingress. Turn it on from the cluster's **Network** settings in the new Ankra portal, with `ankra cluster gateway-api enable` from CLI v0.66.0, or through the API. Values you wrote yourself are never changed. You can also choose the option when you create a cluster.

[Turn it on for a cluster that already exists →](/guides/gateway-api#turn-it-on-for-a-cluster-that-already-exists)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-05" tags={["AI", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "A Teams channel the Ankra bot is already in can be picked for notifications" }}>
## A Teams channel the Ankra bot is already in can be picked for notifications

Some organisations had the Ankra bot in a Microsoft Teams team and still saw no channels when adding a notification destination, so pipeline and alert notifications to that channel needed a Workflows webhook. A team is listed when the bot is added to it, and now also once the bot receives a message there. If your channels are missing, mention **@Ankra AI** once in the team and reload.

[If something does not work →](/platform/ai-connections#if-something-does-not-work)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-05" tags={["Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "Manage and create organisations in the new Ankra portal" }}>
## Manage and create organisations in the new Ankra portal

The new portal now has its own **Organisations** page under **Your account**. It lists every organisation you belong to with your role in each, lets you switch between them, and lets you create a new one. Until now **All organisations** in the organisation picker opened Classic Ankra in a new tab, and creating an organisation meant going to Classic Ankra yourself.

[Switching and creating organisations →](/concepts/organisations#switching-and-creating-organisations)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-05" tags={["CLI", "API"]} rss={{ title: "One registry robot for several projects, and change its projects later" }}>
## One registry robot for several projects, and change its projects later

A registry robot can now reach several projects, so a consumer of charts and images in separate projects needs one pull secret. You can also change what it reaches later. Where the registry cannot do that in place, the robot gets a new secret, and Ankra asks first.

[Changing which projects a robot reaches →](/guides/harbor-registry#changing-which-projects-a-robot-reaches)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-04" tags={["Security", "API"]} rss={{ title: "Security Center reads cover the project you act in" }}>
## Security Center reads cover the project you act in

In an organisation with projects, the Security Center, its API and `ankra security` read the project you act in. An organisation-wide member who relied on a read without a project to see every cluster now sees one project. Choose **All projects** in the new Ankra portal, pass `project_id=all` on the API, or add `--all-projects` to most `ankra security` reads (CLI 0.65.0 or later). Pinned tokens and members limited to projects cannot widen a read.

[Which project a read covers →](/guides/cluster-security#which-project-a-read-covers)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-04" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Put Ankra sign-in in front of a tunnel, for your organisation's members or one project's" }}>
## Put Ankra sign-in in front of a tunnel, for your organisation's members or one project's

A tunnel hostname in a private zone now admits only your organisation's active members, on any email domain, with no OAuth proxy to run. `--access-project` limits a zone to the members who can reach one project. The flag is in the ankra CLI from v0.64.0.

[Protect a tunnel with Ankra sign-in →](/guides/protect-a-tunnel-with-ankra-sign-in)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-04" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Project members can read their project's managed services, and each project sees only its own" }}>
## Project members can read their project's managed services, and each project sees only its own

In closed beta: a managed service now belongs to its cluster's project. A project member with the permission can read that project's services, which used to be refused. An organisation-wide member sees the selected project's services instead of every project's, and a token with no project set sees the Default project's.

[Managed services and projects →](/concepts/managed-services#managed-services-and-projects)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-04" tags={["GitOps", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Decide a diverged GitOps file from the CLI" }}>
## Decide a diverged GitOps file from the CLI

`ankra cluster gitops preserved` shows and decides the files you edited in Git that Ankra keeps instead of overwriting. `list` and `show` print where Git's and Ankra's versions differ, key by key, and `resolve` with `--keep git` or `--keep ankra` records which version wins, after a preview of where the two versions differ. It needs CLI v0.59.0 or later; before, deciding a file took the API.

[Diverged files →](/concepts/gitops#diverged-files)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-03" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "Each project's notifications and AI board threads can go to its own Slack or Teams channel" }}>
## Each project's notifications and AI board threads can go to its own Slack or Teams channel

A notification route in a project now delivers only that project's notifications; an organisation-wide route still hears everything. Routes from before projects stay organisation-wide; one created since without a choice is in the project it was created in, so check those. Board threads can bind a channel per project too, with the organisation's channel as the fallback.

[One channel per project →](/guides/notification-routing#example-one-channel-per-project)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-03" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "The merge train takes stacked pull requests" }}>
## The merge train takes stacked pull requests

A pull request based on another pull request's branch can join the merge train while that pull request is in it. It is tested on top of the pull request below it, a stack inside one window is tested by one run, and the train retargets each one onto the base branch just before merging it.

[Stacked pull requests →](/pipelines/merge-train#stacked-pull-requests)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-02" tags={["Pipelines", "AI"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra AI also fixes a failed test step on a pull request" }}>
## Ankra AI also fixes a failed test step on a pull request

A failed `run` step (your tests, or any script the pipeline runs) on the setup pull request or on a pull request from a branch of your repository now gets the same fix as a failed build or image gate: Ankra AI commits the fix to that branch and watches the run its commit starts; before, a failing test reached nobody.

[When a pull request fails its checks →](/concepts/applications#when-a-pull-request-fails-its-checks)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-01" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Concurrency flags take an expression, so pull requests supersede old runs while pushes always finish" }}>
## Concurrency flags take an expression, so pull requests supersede old runs while pushes always finish

`cancel_in_progress` and `cancel_queued` now accept one `${{ }}` expression, settled for each run. One pipeline can let a pull request's new commit supersede its old run, while a push to your default branch always finishes and keeps only its newest waiting commit. It reads what is known before a run starts: the event, the commit and declared manual inputs. Anything it cannot settle counts as false, and `ankra pipeline validate` warns about it.

[A concurrency policy per event →](/guides/pipeline-reference#a-concurrency-policy-per-event)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-11-01" tags={["API"]} rss={{ title: "Disconnect one application from a managed service that keeps running for the others" }}>
## Disconnect one application from a managed service that keeps running for the others

In closed beta: you can take one application environment off a managed service without deleting the service. A review comes first, and says that the shared credential is not revoked. Nothing changes until the application's next deploy, which rolls its workloads off the connection. Ankra then removes the connection Secret once no pod reads it and, unless you keep it, the namespace's network access. Available in the new Ankra portal, the CLI, the API, the AI Assistant and MCP.

[Disconnect an Application from a Managed Service →](/guides/disconnect-an-application-from-a-managed-service)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-31" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Add an application environment on a new cluster, copied from one you already run" }}>
## Add an application environment on a new cluster, copied from one you already run

In the new Ankra portal, **Add** beside an application's environments now offers **On a new cluster…**: Ankra creates the cluster with a copy of the stacks the environment's cluster runs, then deploys the application there as a new environment with the same image and settings and an address of its own. Secret overrides can come along; data does not.

[Add an environment on another cluster →](/guides/application-environments#add-an-environment-on-another-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-31" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra leaves your own VMs, networks and storage alone on a shared Proxmox VE" }}>
## Ankra leaves your own VMs, networks and storage alone on a shared Proxmox VE

You can connect a Proxmox VE installation that already runs your own VMs. Ankra only adds what it needs next to them. When saving the credential would touch your networks or storage, it stops before changing anything. Creating a cluster clones only Ankra's own template and never overcommits the memory on your hosts. Every VM Ankra creates is tagged `ankra` so you can tell it apart from yours.

[Connecting a Proxmox VE that already runs VMs →](/platform/credentials/proxmox#connecting-a-proxmox-ve-that-already-runs-vms)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-30" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra AI is in the Microsoft Teams Store" }}>
## Ankra AI is in the Microsoft Teams Store

Ankra AI is now published on the Microsoft Teams Store. In Teams, open **Apps**, search for **Ankra AI** and press **Add**, or use **Get it from the Teams Store** and **Open in Teams** on the portal's Microsoft Teams card. A tenant that restricts Store apps needs a Teams administrator to allow it once under **Manage apps**, and the app package stays available as a fallback for tenants that block Store apps entirely.

[Ankra AI on the Microsoft Teams Store →](https://marketplace.microsoft.com/en-us/product/teams-app/WA200012870)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-30" tags={["GitOps", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "The GitOps status shows the commit a cluster is in step with, and when each diverged file was found" }}>
## The GitOps status shows the commit a cluster is in step with, and when each diverged file was found

The GitOps status now shows the commit a cluster is in step with and the Git head Ankra last read, with times, and each listed preserved file's detection time and decision; `ankra cluster gitops status` prints them with CLI v0.52.2 or later. The last sync time can read days old on a current cluster, because a commit that leaves its directory unchanged moves the applied commit without a sync. Deciding a file still takes the API.

[Applied Commit and Git Head →](/concepts/gitops#applied-commit-and-git-head)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-30" tags={["AI", "Cost"]} rss={{ title: "The DevOps AI model by Ankra: $50 a month on every plan, and the model that answers when you run out" }}>
## The DevOps AI model by Ankra: $50 a month on every plan, and the model that answers when you run out

Every catalog now carries **DevOps AI**, Ankra's own model, served on Ankra's own servers with the Ankra CLI skills preloaded. Every plan, the free tier included, gets $50 a month on it, separate from the free monthly allowance; past that it bills per token. When the free allowance or your credit runs out, AI features switch to the DevOps AI model instead of stopping. Pick it in the model picker to use it on purpose.

[The DevOps AI model by Ankra →](/platform/ai-provider#the-devops-ai-model-by-ankra)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-30" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "PersistentVolumes work on kubeadm Proxmox VE clusters" }}>
## PersistentVolumes work on kubeadm Proxmox VE clusters

A `PersistentVolumeClaim` on a kubeadm Proxmox VE cluster now gets a disk on your Proxmox storage, attached to the VM that runs the pod. Nodes register with the providerID and topology labels the bundled CSI needs, and the driver talks to the API root. Nodes created before the fix need replacing, and the API token needs `Datastore.Audit`.

[Persistent Volumes on Proxmox VE Clusters →](/guides/proxmox-persistent-volumes)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-28" tags={["GitOps", "Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "A GitOps sync that would remove much of a cluster waits for you, and a migration re-run names each database it replaces" }}>
## A GitOps sync that would remove much of a cluster waits for you, and a migration re-run names each database it replaces

A GitOps sync that would remove a large share of a cluster, such as two of its four stacks, applies nothing until you remove them through Ankra. A restore into a database Ankra already restored into needs `--overwrite-database`, so the `ankra migrate up` cutover names each one. A stopped cluster's state snapshot is kept while it stays stopped. Ankra AI, MCP and service accounts can schedule a scale-to-zero stop only on a cluster a person confirmed as non-production.

[Large Removals Are Held →](/concepts/gitops#large-removals-are-held)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-28" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Resize the only control plane of a running Hetzner cluster, with a few minutes of API downtime" }}>
## Resize the only control plane of a running Hetzner cluster, with a few minutes of API downtime

A Hetzner cluster with one controller no longer has to be stopped to change the controller's server type. Ankra resizes it in place: the Kubernetes API is down for a few minutes while the server reboots, and workloads on the workers keep running. You accept the downtime first, with **Resize with API downtime** in the dashboard or `--accept-api-downtime` on the CLI. Other providers still need three controllers or a stopped cluster.

[Change the control plane instance type →](/guides/operate-ankra-managed-clusters#change-the-control-plane-instance-type)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-28" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "A guide to running Ankra on Talos Linux clusters" }}>
## A guide to running Ankra on Talos Linux clusters

A new guide sets out what Talos owns - the nodes, the OS and the control plane - and what Ankra adds above the Kubernetes API, from Stacks and GitOps to backups, access and AI operations. It imports a Talos cluster, declares its first Stack, and covers Pod Security admission and the CNI.

[Talos Linux Clusters →](/guides/talos-clusters)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-28" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "A guide to building Bazel repositories on Ankra Pipelines" }}>
## A guide to building Bazel repositories on Ankra Pipelines

A new guide runs Bazel on Ankra Pipelines: Bazelisk and the output tree on the shared workspace, a disk and repository cache kept between runs, JUnit results from `bazel-testlogs`, an optional remote cache, and a Bazel-built binary scanned, gated and published as an image.

[Bazel →](/guides/bazel-pipelines)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-28" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "The Hetzner guide covers the payment method, SSH key and server types you need first" }}>
## The Hetzner guide covers the payment method, SSH key and server types you need first

The Hetzner Clusters guide now says up front that creating a Hetzner cluster needs a payment method on the organisation and an SSH key credential, and walks through the new portal's wizard at **Infrastructure → Clusters → Add a cluster → Hetzner**. Its examples use `cpx12` and `cpx22`, because the `cx` line is often unavailable in `fsn1` and other locations; check what a location sells with `ankra cluster hetzner server-types` first.

[Hetzner Clusters →](/guides/hetzner-clusters#before-you-start)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-28" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "A new film walks through a Hetzner cluster from create to an app over TLS" }}>
## A new film walks through a Hetzner cluster from create to an app over TLS

The Hetzner Clusters guide has a new video: a real cluster in `fsn1` with one `cpx32` control plane and two `cpx22` workers, created from the dashboard and the CLI. All three nodes were Ready five minutes after the create call, and a small stack then answered on the cluster's own hostname with a Let's Encrypt certificate.

[Hetzner Clusters →](/guides/hetzner-clusters#create-the-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-28" tags={["Clusters", "Cost"]} rss={{ title: "Projects: give each team its own room inside your organisation" }}>
## Projects: give each team its own room inside your organisation

Every organisation on the new portal now has projects. A project holds its own clusters, applications, pipelines and cost; people, roles and the invoice stay organisation-wide. Everything you have is in your Default project, so nothing changes until you create a second one. Then move resources into it, invite **Project only** members who see nothing else, read cost by project, and use `ankra project` and `--project` from the CLI.

[Projects →](/concepts/projects)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-27" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "With no AI allowance left, Ankra upgrades vulnerable Python packages on a pull request itself" }}>
## With no AI allowance left, Ankra upgrades vulnerable Python packages on a pull request itself

When the image gate blocks a pull request on vulnerable Python packages and your organisation's AI allowance is used up, Ankra no longer waits for the allowance. It moves each package a lock file pins to a release that fixes it, checks the new pins against the packages' own requirements, and commits that to the pull request on GitHub. The run that commit starts checks the result, and anything left waits for the allowance.

[When a pull request fails its checks →](/concepts/applications#when-a-pull-request-fails-its-checks)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-27" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Kubernetes upgrades have a guide with tested resume and recovery paths" }}>
## Kubernetes upgrades have a guide with tested resume and recovery paths

A new guide covers upgrading Kubernetes on clusters Ankra provisions: measured timings per node, the restore points that exist, and a pre-upgrade checklist. It shows how to resume a hop a PodDisruptionBudget or a cancel stopped with `ankra cluster operations retry`, how Ankra rolls back a failed control plane, and how to rebuild a cluster from its off-cluster state with a stop and a restoring start, each tested end to end.

[Upgrade Kubernetes on a Cluster →](/guides/kubernetes-upgrades)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-27" tags={["Pipelines", "API"]} rss={{ title: "Each application environment can follow its own branches, and a pipeline can see where a push deploys" }}>
## Each application environment can follow its own branches, and a pipeline can see where a push deploys

Give an environment a branch rule and pushes to matching branches deploy there: production follows `main`, development `develop` or every branch except `main`, staging `release/*`. Rules use the same patterns as `when.branches`, and changing one is never itself a deploy. In a pipeline, `ankra.environments` lists the environments the run's push deploys to, so a stage can run only on its way to production.

[Environments and branch rules →](/guides/application-environments)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-26" tags={["Pipelines", "AI"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra AI fixes a failing pull request on its own branch, merge conflicts included" }}>
## Ankra AI fixes a failing pull request on its own branch, merge conflicts included

A failed build or security gate on a pull request from a branch of your repository is now fixed on that pull request: Ankra AI commits the fix to its branch and watches the new run, with no second pull request. A pull request that conflicts with its base gets a merge commit that resolves it. Forks still get a diagnosis. With the AI allowance used up, the pull request says when the fix will start.

[When a pull request fails its checks →](/concepts/applications#when-a-pull-request-fails-its-checks)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-26" tags={["Clusters", "API"]} rss={{ title: "A managed service's connection reaches its application without a redeploy" }}>
## A managed service's connection reaches its application without a redeploy

In closed beta: once a managed service is ready, Ankra writes its connection Secret into the namespace of each application it serves on the same cluster, without redeploying or restarting anything, while no running pod of the application references it. When running pods already reference it, the application's next deploy writes it and rolls them together. The API's new `connection_delivery.pickup` says whether an application needs a deploy or a restart to read it.

[Connect to a Managed Service →](/guides/connect-to-a-managed-service#how-your-application-receives-the-connection)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-26" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Importing an application keeps the CI you already have" }}>
## Importing an application keeps the CI you already have

When the repository you import already builds and pushes its image, from GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, Bitbucket Pipelines or CircleCI, Ankra now adopts that pipeline: it deploys the image your CI publishes and the setup pull request adds no second pipeline or Dockerfile. CI that only runs tests is kept. The analysis also tells you when your CI is not starting, and how to fix it.

[When your repository already has CI →](/concepts/applications#when-your-repository-already-has-ci)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-24" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "The welcome credit is now €59 for 60 days" }}>
## The welcome credit is now €59 for 60 days

New organisations now unlock €59 of welcome credit when they save a payment method, instead of €249. The credit is still valid for 60 days. New Ankra Cloud accounts receive the same €59 sign-up credit. Credit that was granted before this change keeps its original amount and expiry date.

[Welcome credit →](/platform/billing#welcome-credit)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-23" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "The merge train keeps testing while GitHub declines a merge" }}>
## The merge train keeps testing while GitHub declines a merge

When GitHub declines the first car's merge, for example because a required check is still running, the pull requests behind it now keep building and testing instead of waiting. A merge declined because a required check failed leaves the train after 15 minutes instead of three hours, so one red check no longer holds the train.

[The merge train →](/pipelines/merge-train#merges-github-declines)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-22" tags={["Pipelines", "API"]} rss={{ title: "Approve a portability or next.config pull request in the Launch Plan and Ankra opens it" }}>
## Approve a portability or next.config pull request in the Launch Plan and Ankra opens it

A portability pull request, or the next.config pull request that moves your `vercel.json` routing into `next.config`, is now an action you can approve in the Launch Plan. Open it once under your approval and Ankra opens it on your repository at the plan's commit. If Ankra cannot make the change with confidence, it says why a person has to, and opens nothing. Ankra never merges these pull requests.

[Portability pull requests →](/use-cases/vercel-to-ankra#portability-pull-requests)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-22" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Move a pull request to the front of the merge train" }}>
## Move a pull request to the front of the merge train

With CLI 0.47.0 or later, an organisation administrator can put a pull request at the front of a repository's merge train with `ankra pipeline train front <pull request>`. It goes ahead of every pull request that is still waiting or being tested, and those are tested again on top of it. Pull requests that already passed or are being merged stay ahead.

[The merge train →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#the-merge-train-test-every-pull-request-as-it-will-merge)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-21" tags={["AI", "API"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra AI acts on an approved Launch Plan without asking again" }}>
## Ankra AI acts on an approved Launch Plan without asking again

Once you approve a Launch Plan, Ankra AI runs the approved actions without asking you again: in your chat, in an agent run, or over MCP. Anything outside the plan still asks first. Each approved action runs once. The approval stops working when it expires, when you revoke it, or when the plan changes. Only your own runs can use it.

[MCP server →](/platform/mcp-server#how-calls-behave)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-21" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Move a vendor's data into your cluster under the Launch Plan's one approval, with a rollback window" }}>
## Move a vendor's data into your cluster under the Launch Plan's one approval, with a rollback window

In closed beta: when you move a service into your cluster, the Launch Plan now carries its data migration. Postgres moves by dump and restore through a backup vault, and S3-compatible object storage is copied with checksums. At one cutover the vendor sources go read-only and the last changes are copied, then your app's variables switch in one change. During a rollback window, `ankra application data-migration rollback` puts everything back. Ankra never deletes data at the vendor.

[Moving your data →](/use-cases/vercel-to-ankra#moving-your-data-closed-beta)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-21" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "The proof of a Vercel project's setup pull request is compared with Vercel's own preview" }}>
## The proof of a Vercel project's setup pull request is compared with Vercel's own preview

For a Vercel project, the proof run now requests the same routes on Vercel's preview of the commit as on the ankra.cc preview. It compares status, content type, JSON keys or page title and landmarks, and size. The proof card marks each route as match, differs (with the reason) or unknown. Unknown is never counted as a match, and a difference is a warning that does not fail the proof.

[Compared with your Vercel preview →](/use-cases/vercel-to-ankra#compared-with-your-vercel-preview)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-20" tags={["Pipelines", "AI"]} rss={{ title: "The PR shepherd drives the pull requests Ankra opens to ready, and merges them only if you opt in" }}>
## The PR shepherd drives the pull requests Ankra opens to ready, and merges them only if you opt in

An **Ankra PR shepherd** check now follows each setup pull request. When your CI never starts, Ankra runs its own pipeline, build and proof on the head. It merges the base branch in when the pull request falls behind, resolves conflicts only in Ankra's own files, and fixes its own failures up to 3 times. It merges only where you opted in, per organisation or per application.

[The PR shepherd →](/concepts/applications#the-pr-shepherd)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-20" tags={["Pipelines", "GitOps"]} rss={{ title: "Vercel to Ankra: one Launch Plan, one approval, and your crons and variables come along" }}>
## Vercel to Ankra: one Launch Plan, one approval, and your crons and variables come along

On Vercel and GitHub pages, the Chrome extension now shows where your project stands and one Launch Plan to approve. Bring variables over from Vercel. In the setup pull request:

- A standalone Next.js build ships as just its server.
- Vercel crons become CronJobs.
- Rewrites and headers are listed with where to move them.

[Vercel to Ankra →](/use-cases/vercel-to-ankra)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-20" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "A run handed to Ankra platform builders is now scanned, gated and published" }}>
## A run handed to Ankra platform builders is now scanned, gated and published

When a cluster's agent is too old to run pipeline steps and the build goes to Ankra platform builders, its scans now run on Ankra's platform scanner instead of being skipped. The image gate judges them under the same policy, and the image is published when the gate approves. If the scanner is unavailable, the scan fails with the reason and nothing is published.

[Ankra platform builders →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#ankra-platform-builders)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-19" tags={["Clusters", "Cost"]} rss={{ title: "Guided setup takes a new organisation from a free cluster to a live website" }}>
## Guided setup takes a new organisation from a free cluster to a live website

Four steps: create a free cluster, add a payment method to unlock €249 of credit for 60 days, choose the Ankra default or a new GitHub repository, then describe a website in one sentence and Ankra deploys it with a public address. Welcome credit now comes with a saved card, not at sign-up, and you are never charged without a top-up. Playgrounds are moving to Ankra Cloud dev clusters: free for 24 hours, kept with a card.

[Choose where to start →](/get-started/start-here)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-19" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "New use case: self-host an AI agent observability backend from one shared stack profile" }}>
## New use case: self-host an AI agent observability backend from one shared stack profile

A vendor can now hand its whole backend to customers as one shared stack profile. The new use case walks a platform engineer through Moyai's: the trace API, highly available Postgres, S3, an Iceberg REST catalog, an LLM and Prometheus, applied to Proxmox, HPE Morpheus, an imported cluster or any cloud with a dry run, secret files and one deploy, then upgraded with a rollout.

[Self-host an AI agent observability backend on-prem →](/use-cases/self-host-ai-agent-observability)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-17" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "A card is required to create resources on Ankra Cloud whatever credit you hold, and automatic top-up stays off until you turn it on" }}>
## A card is required to create resources on Ankra Cloud whatever credit you hold, and automatic top-up stays off until you turn it on

On Ankra Cloud, creating a resource now needs a card on file, whatever credit the account holds. Organisations Ankra invoices under a custom agreement need no card. Credit is still spent first, and the card pays only the rest. On the Ankra platform, saving a card does not switch automatic top-up on: you turn it on under **Billing** with your own threshold, amount and daily and monthly caps, and Billing warns you while it is not on.

[Credit and payment method →](/platform/billing#credit-and-payment-method)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-17" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "A cancelled run now runs its finally stages before it concludes" }}>
## A cancelled run now runs its finally stages before it concludes

Cancelling a run that had started now stops its main and `on_failure` stages, then runs its `finally` stages before the run concludes `cancelled`, so cleanup happens after a cancel too. This replaces the 2026-10-16 note that a cancelled run skipped them. `ankra pipeline cancel --force` skips the cleanup and concludes the run at once.

[Cancelling a run →](/guides/pipeline-reference#cancelling-a-run)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-17" tags={["AI", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "List and clear what Ankra's AI remembers about you from the CLI" }}>
## List and clear what Ankra's AI remembers about you from the CLI

`ankra ai memories list` shows the memories Ankra's AI has saved for you in the current organisation, newest first, and `ankra ai memories clear` deletes all of them after a confirmation. Both act on your own memories only. They need the ankra CLI v0.40.0 or later.

[AI Memory and Feedback →](/platform/ai-memory)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-17" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Security patches, Kubernetes patch upgrades and other routine changes are recorded as pre-approved standard changes" }}>
## Security patches, Kubernetes patch upgrades and other routine changes are recorded as pre-approved standard changes

The audit log now marks routine changes as pre-approved standard changes for ISO 27001 change management, naming the catalogue entry and why each qualified: security-advisory add-on upgrades, Kubernetes patch upgrades, autoscaling, scheduled power actions, access expiry and token rotation. Security upgrades also name the advisories they fix in their GitOps commit. Pre-approval is on by default; admins can turn it off under **Organisation settings > Security**.

[Change Management →](/security/change-management)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-17" tags={["GitOps", "Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Manage everything the CLI can create from Terraform with provider 0.3.0" }}>
## Manage everything the CLI can create from Terraform with provider 0.3.0

The Ankra Terraform provider now covers what the Ankra CLI can create, change or delete: clusters on every cloud, managed Kubernetes, node groups, credentials, Helm registries, variables, cluster settings, alerts, DNS, applications, pipelines, stack profiles, backups, members, tokens and organisation settings. Set `project_id` on the provider if your organisation uses projects.

[Ankra Terraform Provider →](/integrations/terraform)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-17" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Run every stage when you need to, keep chosen stages from ever being skipped, and skip a push with [skip ci]" }}>
## Run every stage when you need to, keep chosen stages from ever being skipped, and skip a push with [skip ci]

Path filters can now be set aside. Run every stage with `--full` on `ankra pipeline run` or `rerun`, or add the `ankra:full-run` label to a GitHub pull request. A `full_run` block can run every push to main in full, and each full run records why. Your organisation can name stages a pull request can never filter away. A pushed commit with `[skip ci]` records a skipped run, except on the default branch by default.

[Setting path filters aside →](/guides/pipeline-reference#setting-path-filters-aside)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-16" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "on_failure stages now run only when a run fails, and finally stages run after a failure too" }}>
## on_failure stages now run only when a run fails, and finally stages run after a failure too

An `on_failure` stage ran after a green run and was skipped after a failed one, and a `finally` stage was skipped whenever the run failed. Now an `on_failure` stage runs only when a main stage failed, and a `finally` stage runs whatever the outcome. A run cancelled as a whole does not run its `finally` stages yet.

[Failure and cleanup stages →](/guides/pipeline-reference#failure-and-cleanup-stages)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-14" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Applications can refuse images that did not come from your pipeline gate" }}>
## Applications can refuse images that did not come from your pipeline gate

Set an application to `warn` or `enforce` and each deploy checks that its images carry a signed statement from your organisation's pipeline. Manage the mode and your signing keys in the UI or with `ankra application provenance` and `ankra org provenance`.

[Verified provenance at deploy →](/security/deploy-provenance)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-14" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "Chat with Ankra and deploy from inside Lovable with the Chrome extension" }}>
## Chat with Ankra and deploy from inside Lovable with the Chrome extension

The Ankra - Chat & Deploy extension for Chrome adds an **Ask Ankra** button to your Lovable projects. Chat with Ankra AI, connect the project's GitHub repository, and deploy it to your cluster without leaving Lovable. If your organisation has no cluster yet, it can request a temporary Playground for you. While the Chrome Web Store listing is in review, download it from ankra.ai and load it into Chrome yourself.

[Chat and deploy from inside Lovable →](/use-cases/lovable-to-ankra#chat-and-deploy-from-inside-lovable)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-13" tags={["Pipelines", "AI"]} rss={{ title: "A new application's setup pull request now fixes itself when its checks fail" }}>
## A new application's setup pull request now fixes itself when its checks fail

When the setup pull request fails its build or security checks, often on dependencies the repository already had, Ankra AI commits a fix to that same pull request and its status comment says so in plain words. Creating the application from the portal also approves the generated pipeline in your name, so the first runs are not held for an approval.

[Applications →](/concepts/applications#when-the-setup-pull-request-does-not-pass-its-checks)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-11" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Run your tests and lint in Ankra Workspaces instead of on your laptop" }}>
## Run your tests and lint in Ankra Workspaces instead of on your laptop

`ankra exec` runs a command for your checkout, uncommitted changes included, in a warm pod on your organisation's CI pool and streams the output back. `ankra dev install` routes the heavy commands you and your coding agents already type - `go test`, `golangci-lint`, `pnpm typecheck` - there automatically. A repository whose pipeline runs elsewhere can be connected for Workspaces only.

[Run tests and lint in Ankra Workspaces →](/guides/ankra-workspaces)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-10" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "The merge train: every pull request is tested as it will merge" }}>
## The merge train: every pull request is tested as it will merge

Turn on a repository's merge train and Ankra tests each queued pull request squashed onto the base branch and the pull requests ahead of it, then merges it through GitHub when that run passes. A failing pull request leaves the train and the rest are retested without it. Every merge is a tree a run already passed, so `promote_from: pull_request` publishes the tested image instead of rebuilding. GitHub only.

[The merge train →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#the-merge-train-test-every-pull-request-as-it-will-merge)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-09" tags={["API", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Remove an application from one cluster and keep it everywhere else" }}>
## Remove an application from one cluster and keep it everywhere else

**Settings** → **Park or remove** takes an application off one cluster while its settings, secrets, images, release history and other deployments stay. Its data goes only once a restore point from the last day covers it; otherwise nothing is removed and the reason says what to back up. A production deployment, or the last place it runs, asks for the cluster's name. Also `POST /api/v1/org/applications/{application_id}/deployments/remove`, `ankra application remove <application> --cluster <cluster>` and the `remove_application_deployment` tool. Needs CLI v0.29.0.

[Remove a deployment from one cluster →](/concepts/applications#remove-a-deployment-from-one-cluster)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-09" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "A repository Ankra cannot build no longer gets a setup pull request" }}>
## A repository Ankra cannot build no longer gets a setup pull request

Setup now stops before the pull request when a repository holds nothing that keeps running, or no Dockerfile that Ankra found or could write. It used to open a pull request that could never deploy a working application. The setup ends as failed, its message says what Ankra found and what to add, and it ends: No setup pull request was opened and nothing was deployed.

[How it works →](/concepts/applications#how-it-works)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-09" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Promote a pull request's tested image instead of rebuilding on merge" }}>
## Promote a pull request's tested image instead of rebuilding on merge

A publish stage can now declare `promote_from: pull_request`. When a merge carries exactly the files a pull request run already built, scanned, gated and tested, the merge publishes that run's approved image as its own `sha-<7>` tag instead of building and testing it again. Any other merge builds in full as before, and the run says why.

[Promote a tested image instead of rebuilding →](/guides/pipeline-reference#promote-a-tested-image-instead-of-rebuilding)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-09" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Pipeline steps record what they used, and each stage gets a resource suggestion" }}>
## Pipeline steps record what they used, and each stage gets a resource suggestion

Every step now records its memory peak and CPU use next to what it asked for, so a stage that declares 14Gi and peaks at 8 GiB shows on the run itself. The API suggests `resources.memory` and `resources.cpu` per stage from its latest successful runs, and says how many runs each suggestion rests on. Suggestions are advice only: your pipeline changes only if you change it.

[Capacity and concurrency →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#capacity-and-concurrency)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-09" tags={["Security", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Acknowledge or accept findings no add-on owns, per workload or per image" }}>
## Acknowledge or accept findings no add-on owns, per workload or per image

A disposition needed a finding attributed to an add-on, so findings in operator-managed database pods, plain manifests, control-plane pods and charts outside the catalogue could never be acknowledged or accepted. `ankra security dispositions preview|create --scope workload` pins the decision to one workload, and `--scope image` to an image digest wherever it runs. The same `scope` works from the API and the MCP tools.

[What a disposition is pinned to →](/guides/cluster-security#acknowledge-or-accept-the-risk)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-09" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Generated pipelines keep their package caches between runs" }}>
## Generated pipelines keep their package caches between runs

A new application's generated test stage now keeps its package caches: npm, pnpm or Yarn, Go modules and builds, pip, uv, Poetry or Pipenv, and cargo. Each is keyed on the lock file and saved only after the step passes. A pull request starts warm from the default branch and never writes into it. `fallback: none` keeps a cache off shared volumes on older agents.

[Caches →](/guides/pipeline-reference#caches)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-09" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "UpCloud node groups: a plan from another family replaces the nodes instead of stranding them" }}>
## UpCloud node groups: a plan from another family replaces the nodes instead of stranding them

UpCloud changes a server's plan in place only within its plan family, so resizing a STARTER group to a general-purpose plan such as `4xCPU-8GB` was refused after each node had been stopped, and the retries left the nodes off. A cross-family change now replaces each node with the new plan and the resize says so, a control-plane change across families is refused up front, and a server whose in-place resize fails is powered back on.

[Node groups →](/platform/cluster-nodes#change-a-node-group)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-09" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Run every pipeline pod on a dedicated node group" }}>
## Run every pipeline pod on a dedicated node group

`ankra cluster agent ci set --node-group pipelines` puts every pipeline pod of the CI cluster on that node group: step pods, their cache and artifact helpers, and image builds select its nodes and tolerate its taints, with no pipeline change and no Kyverno policy. The portal's Pipelines settings page edits the placement, the CI slots and the storage class.

[Step placement →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#step-placement)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-08" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra Cloud dev clusters: Kubernetes on one server" }}>
## Ankra Cloud dev clusters: Kubernetes on one server

Ankra Cloud Kubernetes can create a dev cluster: one server running k3s that is the control plane, the only node and its own network edge. It has no control-plane fee, so you pay for the server. It has no high availability and is not for production. Pick **Dev cluster on one server** in **Create Cluster**, or pass `--dev-cluster` to `ankra cluster managed create` (CLI v0.24.0). It is offered zone by zone, and Ankra Cloud is in closed beta.

[Dev clusters →](/guides/ankra-cloud-kubernetes#dev-clusters)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-08" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Path filters per event: check a pull request's component, build everything on push" }}>
## Path filters per event: check a pull request's component, build everything on push

A stage's `when` can name its event: `when.pull_request.paths` filters pull requests, `push: {}` leaves pushes unfiltered, so a monorepo needs one stage per component, not duplicates. A stage whose `if:` calls `always()` or `skipped()` now runs past a skipped need. Converted workflows keep their own pull request paths, and `ankra pipeline validate --changed-file` prints each stage against each event.

[Event-scoped filters →](/guides/pipeline-reference#event-scoped-filters)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-08" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "Pull request runs apply their path filters to the files the pull request changes" }}>
## Pull request runs apply their path filters to the files the pull request changes

A pull request run had no list of changed files, so every `when.paths` filter passed and every stage ran. Ankra now reads the changed files from GitHub, GitLab or Bitbucket, up to 300, and keeps them on the run for dispatch and re-runs. A list it cannot read in full never skips a stage, and the run's `paths_unverified` says why.

[How path filters decide →](/guides/pipeline-reference#triggers)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-08" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Every sign-in is bound to the browser that started it" }}>
## Every sign-in is bound to the browser that started it

Every sign-in - password, social account, your organisation's single sign-on provider or an invitation link - now ties the identity provider's answer to the browser that asked for it, with OpenID Connect `state`, `nonce` and PKCE. A response this browser did not start is never completed; the sign-in restarts once instead. Connecting another sign-in method is bound the same way. Day to day nothing changes: if a long-idle tab lands on "sign in again", signing in once more completes it.

[Sign-in and SSO →](/security/identity-and-access#sign-in-and-sso)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-08" tags={["Cost"]} rss={{ title: "See what your organisation used of Ankra, meter by meter" }}>
## See what your organisation used of Ankra, meter by meter

One usage report covers worker vCPU-hours, playgrounds, managed service time and storage, Ankra hosted logs and metrics, and AI, per month or day, split by cluster, application or managed service. Each line says how completely it was measured - a period nobody measured reads as not measured, never 0 - and what it costs. Managed services and hosted logs and metrics are included during the pilot. Read it through the API, the AI Assistant or `ankra org usage` (CLI v0.27.0).

[Usage →](/platform/usage)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-08" tags={["Pipelines", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "Steps of a run stay on the node their workspace is attached to" }}>
## Steps of a run stay on the node their workspace is attached to

Each step of a run, and its cache and artifact helpers, now runs on the node its `rwo` workspace is attached to, so sequential steps no longer wait for the volume to move. When that node is gone, cordoned or not Ready, the step log says why the pin was dropped. Helpers also carry the step's tolerations, so caches restore on a tainted node group. `ankra pipeline get` shows where each step ran.

[Workspace access →](/guides/pipeline-reference#top-level-keys)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-08" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "A step says whether its caches restored, and why not" }}>
## A step says whether its caches restored, and why not

A step's cache result is now `hit` only when every cache restored, `partial` when some did, and `restore_failed` when a restore broke, each cache carrying a reason such as `download_failed`. `ankra pipeline get` shows it in a CACHE column, `--step <key>` prints the per-cache table, and the run timeline shows a chip. Where a step's network allows it, the restore runs inside the step's own pod.

[What a step reports about its caches →](/guides/pipeline-reference#what-a-step-reports-about-its-caches)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-08" tags={["Clusters", "Cost"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra Cloud Kubernetes: development and production control planes" }}>
## Ankra Cloud Kubernetes: development and production control planes

An Ankra Cloud Kubernetes cluster now has a control plane you choose at create. **Production** runs three replicas, is highly available and costs €40 a month per cluster. **Development** runs one replica and has no control-plane fee, so you pay for the nodes only. It is not for production: an upgrade or a host node failure interrupts the Kubernetes API while it restarts. The choice cannot be changed afterwards.

[Choosing a control plane →](/guides/ankra-cloud-kubernetes#choosing-a-control-plane)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-07" tags={["Cost", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "See how full your image registry is, choose what it keeps, and ask for more" }}>
## See how full your image registry is, choose what it keeps, and ask for more

Every registry project has a storage limit, 50 GiB by default, and a full one refuses every push. **Organisation settings** → **Registry storage** and `ankra registry storage` show usage against the limit and which repositories take the space. You set retention yourself: an organisation policy, rules per repository pattern and a cleanup you can start now, or ask Ankra for a bigger limit, which it reviews. The platform default keeps 30 days and the 3 newest images per repository.

[Registry storage →](/guides/cicd-pipeline#registry-storage)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-07" tags={["GitOps", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "List and resolve GitOps conflicts from the CLI or with an API token" }}>
## List and resolve GitOps conflicts from the CLI or with an API token

`ankra cluster gitops conflicts list` shows the resources that changed in both Git and the platform since they last agreed, and `resolve` with `--keep git` or `--keep cluster` records which side wins, for one conflict or for all of them with `--all`. A script with an API token can call the same routes. Any member of the organisation can list conflicts; resolving needs `clusters.write` on the cluster. It needs CLI v0.24.0 or later.

[When Git and the platform both change →](/concepts/gitops#when-git-and-the-platform-both-change)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-07" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "A publish stage can give the published image extra tags, such as latest" }}>
## A publish stage can give the published image extra tags, such as latest

`kind: publish` takes a `publish.tags` list: each entry becomes another tag on the digest the gate approved, in the application's repository, with no second push - a moving `latest`, or `"0.2.${{ ankra.run.number }}"`. Expressions resolve when the run is planned, the stage's `when:` applies, and a tag the registry refuses fails the step by name.

[The publish block →](/guides/pipeline-reference#kind-specific-blocks)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-07" tags={["CLI", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "A declared registry project that does not exist is created, or the declaration is refused" }}>
## A declared registry project that does not exist is created, or the declaration is refused

`ankra application registry set` with `--admin-credential` now checks that the declared project exists on your registry. A missing project is created with that credential - private, or public with `--project-public` - when it may create projects; otherwise the declaration is refused naming the project and host, instead of every later build failing at the push.

[Have the project, or let Ankra create it →](/guides/harbor-registry#what-harbor-needs-first)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-07" tags={["CLI", "API"]} rss={{ title: "Create registry robots on a Harbor you run yourself" }}>
## Create registry robots on a Harbor you run yourself

The **Registry robots** page and `ankra registry robots create` can now create a robot on a Harbor your organisation runs and has connected as an OCI registry. Pick the registry, a credential of yours that may manage robots there, and one of that registry's projects, such as `platform-charts`: the robot reaches only that project. Ankra acts only with your credential.

[Robots on a Harbor you run yourself →](/guides/harbor-registry#robots-on-a-harbor-you-run-yourself)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-06" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Serve a zone you keep at Cloudflare from every cluster, with a connected Cloudflare credential" }}>
## Serve a zone you keep at Cloudflare from every cluster, with a connected Cloudflare credential

`ankra org custom-dns-zones add --zone <zone> --cloudflare-credential <name>` publishes a zone you keep at Cloudflare through external-dns's native Cloudflare provider. Before storing the declaration, Ankra checks with Cloudflare that the token reaches the zone and is not denied DNS edit there. The controller runs upsert-only: a record you made by hand is never changed or deleted. A credential whose token Ankra no longer holds lists as `needs-reconnect`, and connecting again under the same name repairs it. Needs CLI v0.24.0.

[Serve a Cloudflare zone from every cluster →](/guides/use-your-own-domain#serve-a-cloudflare-zone-from-your-clusters)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-06" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "Protecting a CloudNativePG stack now backs it up" }}>
## Protecting a CloudNativePG stack now backs it up

Protecting a stack with a CloudNativePG cluster never gave the `Cluster` its barman-cloud archiver, so every restore point failed with "the cluster has no plugin configured". Protecting the stack, or taking a restore point by hand, now wires the archiver and its object store into the stack, and unprotecting removes both. Wiring it restarts the instances one at a time; a scheduled backup never does, and skips a stack that is not wired yet.

[Protect a stack →](/guides/backups#protect-a-stack)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-05" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "A stack's data inventory lists only what the stack owns, and an in-place restore refuses volumes it does not own" }}>
## A stack's data inventory lists only what the stack owns, and an in-place restore refuses volumes it does not own

`ankra cluster stacks data list`, the API and `list_stack_data_assets` list as the stack's only data a member owns, or data in a namespace nothing else uses. Anything else in its namespaces is listed separately as not owned by this stack: never captured or restored as the stack's, and left out of the total. An in-place restore refuses volumes the stack does not own, even with `--force`. The CLI shows the reason and the separate list from v0.24.0.

[Protect a stack →](/guides/backups#protect-a-stack)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-05" tags={["Security", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "Cluster groups on environment follow each cluster's Environment" }}>
## Cluster groups on environment follow each cluster's Environment

A dynamic cluster group on `environment` now matches the **Environment** set on each cluster. Before, it matched a copy taken when cluster groups launched, so a group on `environment` = `production` missed clusters created or moved to production since then, and kept a cluster moved out of production. Roles assigned over the group, and the Kubernetes access they bundle, now follow a cluster's environment: platform permissions within about a minute, Kubernetes access within about 90 seconds.

[Restrict production to a DevOps role →](/guides/roles-and-access#example-restrict-production-to-a-devops-role)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-05" tags={["Clusters", "AI"]} rss={{ title: "VictoriaLogs is a fourth log source" }}>
## VictoriaLogs is a fourth log source

A cluster's log source can be a VictoriaLogs instance, beside Grafana Loki, Elasticsearch and OpenSearch: `provider: victorialogs` in the cluster document, or **VictoriaLogs** on the Log Source settings page. Searches run as LogsQL, raw queries included; the optional tenant is `<account>` or `<account>:<project>`; field mappings default to `_time`, `_msg` and the `kubernetes.*` names the victoria-logs charts' collector writes. The connection test proves a read-only credential, and Ankra AI's log tools read the store too.

[Supported providers →](/integrations/log-sources#supported-providers)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-05" tags={["Pipelines", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Route one application's pipeline notifications by branch or pull request" }}>
## Route one application's pipeline notifications by branch or pull request

Routing rules gain two filters. **Application** matches only notifications about one application, and a **reference pattern** matches the branch, tag or pull request they are about: `main`, `release/*`, `pull/*` for every pull request. Pipeline run outcomes, approval requests and approvals, the image gate and PR previews carry a reference, and the application when the repository has one. In the CLI, `ankra alerts routes create --application <name> --reference-pattern main`; the preview takes `--application` and `--reference`. Needs CLI v0.24.0.

[Scope a route to one application and its default branch →](/guides/notification-routing#scope-a-route-to-one-application-and-its-default-branch)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-04" tags={["Pipelines", "Security"]} rss={{ title: "Tag runs no longer use shared pipeline caches" }}>
## Tag runs no longer use shared pipeline caches

A pipeline run triggered by a tag, or a rerun of one, now starts with empty cache volumes. Before, it shared a cache scope with feature branches and pull requests, so a release could start from files another branch's run left behind. The step reports `cache_result: disabled`. Other runs keep their caches, and a tag run takes as long as a run with a cold cache.

[How a stage's cache is scoped →](/guides/pipeline-reference#fields-common-to-every-stage)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-04" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "One component can build both an image and a release bundle" }}>
## One component can build both an image and a release bundle

Declare `build.component` on a build stage to name the component it belongs to, so `build-portal-image` can build portal's image beside a `build-portal` release bundle. A component's bundle now publishes to its own `<component>-bundle` repository, so the image and the bundle no longer overwrite each other's commit tag. `ankra pipeline validate` reports a stage that belongs to no component before a run.

[Which component a build belongs to →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#which-component-a-build-belongs-to)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-02" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "See pipeline capacity live, and steps waiting for a CI slot no longer burn their timeout" }}>
## See pipeline capacity live, and steps waiting for a CI slot no longer burn their timeout

The Pipelines settings page shows your CI slots, steps in flight and queued runs, and edits the parallel run and step limits. The run limit now defaults to 16. A queued run can say it is waiting for a CI slot, a step's timeout and duration start when it begins, and steps show their queue wait. Current agents change slot counts live, up to 128, without a restart.

[Capacity and concurrency →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#capacity-and-concurrency)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-02" tags={["Security", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Extra registry projects, and robot accounts bound to the one you pick" }}>
## Extra registry projects, and robot accounts bound to the one you pick

Everything an organisation publishes lands in one registry project, so every robot account could reach all of it. You can now create up to five extra projects with `ankra registry projects create` or from the **Registry robots** page, push the images you want kept apart there, and bind a robot account to that project alone with `--project`. A robot reaches the one project it is bound to and nothing else.

[Scoping a robot to one project →](/guides/harbor-registry#scoping-a-robot-to-one-project)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Secret value digests can no longer be used to check a guessed value" }}>
## Secret value digests can no longer be used to check a guessed value

Without the reveal permission you see each Secret value as a digest, which shows that a value changed. That digest used to be an unkeyed SHA-256 prefix anyone could compare against guesses. It is now keyed per cluster with a key only the platform holds, so it cannot be used to check a guessed value. The AI assistant also finds Secrets only by name, namespace and labels, never by their stored content.

[Configs →](/platform/kubernetes-insights#configuration)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Security", "API"]} rss={{ title: "Helm release values and kubectl Secret reads need the Secret reveal permission" }}>
## Helm release values and kubectl Secret reads need the Secret reveal permission

A Helm release now hides the passwords, tokens and keys in its values, rendered Secrets and notes until a member with `kubernetes.secrets_reveal` clicks **Reveal values**. An upgrade that still carries a hidden placeholder is refused.

`kubectl` through Ankra refuses Secret requests from members without the permission, whatever their access grant. Every Helm reveal and every Secret request it lets through is written to the audit log.

[Secrets need their own permission →](/guides/cluster-access#secrets-need-their-own-permission)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "The Teams claim code is sent in a private chat and no longer changes while you use it" }}>
## The Teams claim code is sent in a private chat and no longer changes while you use it

When **Ankra AI** is added to a team or group chat, it now sends the claim code that connects your Microsoft tenant to the person who added it in a private chat, instead of posting it in the channel. The welcome card shows when the code expires, and messaging the bot again returns the same code until it is close to expiry, so a colleague talking to the bot no longer invalidates the code an admin is about to redeem.

[Connecting Microsoft Teams →](/platform/ai-connections#connecting-microsoft-teams)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Cost", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "The cost estimate prices GPU node groups, starting with UpCloud" }}>
## The cost estimate prices GPU node groups, starting with UpCloud

The what-if draft and **Compare providers** now price GPU node groups. Each group has a **GPUs** field, a GPU group is placed only on a GPU size, and every UpCloud GPU plan can be priced, spot plans included. Providers with no GPU sizes in the price book show the group as not priced instead of quoting a CPU size. The cluster cost card no longer prices UpCloud B200, RTX PRO 6000 and GPU spot nodes at zero.

[What if the node groups changed? →](/platform/cloud-cost#what-if-the-node-groups-changed)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Connect your own identity provider for single sign-on, with nothing to request from Ankra" }}>
## Connect your own identity provider for single sign-on, with nothing to request from Ankra

An owner or admin can now connect the organisation's own OpenID Connect provider - Microsoft Entra ID, Okta or any other - under **Organisation** → **Settings** → **Single sign-on**. The provider decides who is a member and, through group mappings, which role they hold. A member it stops admitting is suspended at the end of the re-verification window, 24 hours by default, which stops their sessions, API tokens and Kubernetes access together.

[Single Sign-On →](/guides/single-sign-on)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "The Credentials page fills the window, and its empty states lead somewhere" }}>
## The Credentials page fills the window, and its empty states lead somewhere

The **Credentials** page now fits the window: the title and the search, filter and sort row stay in place while the cards scroll. With no credentials yet, the page says what they are for and offers a shortcut to each kind (source control, cloud providers, container registries, access keys), opening the provider list at that group. A search or filter that matches nothing offers **Clear filters**.

[Credentials →](/platform/credentials#find-a-credential)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Security", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "Registry robots list Ankra's own robots, and yours can hold exact permissions and expire" }}>
## Registry robots list Ankra's own robots, and yours can hold exact permissions and expire

The **Registry robots** page and `ankra registry robots list` now show every login your registry project honours: the ones you created and the ones Ankra manages (`ci`, `pull`, one per application), read-only. A robot you create can hold exactly the permissions a job needs, such as deleting artifacts or moving tags, instead of push or pull, and can be set to expire. Each robot names the one project it is bound to.

[Robot accounts you create yourself →](/guides/harbor-registry#robot-accounts-you-create-yourself)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "Invitations can be accepted by people who sign in with Microsoft" }}>
## Invitations can be accepted by people who sign in with Microsoft

Someone invited to an organisation who signed in with Microsoft was told their email address was not verified, with no way to verify it. The link in the invitation email now confirms the address when they open it and sign in, so the invitation can be accepted. An invitation sent before this change needs a resend from **Organisation → Members** to carry the new link, and a resend retires any link mailed earlier.

[Roles and access →](/guides/roles-and-access)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Security"]} rss={{ title: "Reading a Kubernetes Secret's values needs its own permission and is audited" }}>
## Reading a Kubernetes Secret's values needs its own permission and is audited

Opening a Secret in Ankra now shows its key names only. To see the values, click **Reveal values**: that needs the new `kubernetes.secrets_reveal` permission, which `owner`, `admin` and `operator` hold and `member` and `viewer` do not, and each reveal is written to the audit log. The API follows the same rule, and the AI never sees the values. Custom roles that held `kubernetes.write` were given the new permission.

[Kubernetes Resources →](/platform/kubernetes-insights#configuration)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Pipeline steps reserve half their CPU, so more of a run fits on one node" }}>
## Pipeline steps reserve half their CPU, so more of a run fits on one node

A step used to reserve the whole CPU figure its stage declared. A run's steps share one node, so that node filled up and the rest of the run waited, whatever the cluster's node count. A step now reserves half of its `resources.cpu` (at least 100m) and can still use all of it while the node has CPU free. Memory is reserved in full, as before. Nothing in a pipeline definition changes.

[Capacity and concurrency →](/guides/ankra-pipelines#capacity-and-concurrency)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Creates and scale-ups that a cluster's pod range cannot hold are refused up front" }}>
## Creates and scale-ups that a cluster's pod range cannot hold are refused up front

A cluster Ankra builds holds at most 64 nodes with the default network ranges. A create, a node group change or an autoscaler scale-up that would go past that is now refused with a message naming the range and the limit, instead of adding nodes that join and never become Ready.

[Network ranges and their limits →](/platform/cluster-mesh#network-ranges-and-their-limits)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-10-01" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra's builders run at most three builds per organisation at a time" }}>
## Ankra's builders run at most three builds per organisation at a time

The builder pool is shared by every organisation. One organisation's burst of builds now waits its turn after three instead of holding every builder, so another organisation's build starts without waiting for it.

[Waiting for a builder →](/concepts/applications#waiting-for-a-builder)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-30" tags={["Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Applications are available to every organisation" }}>
## Applications are available to every organisation

Applications are out of closed beta and on for every organisation, with nothing to request. Connect a GitHub repository and Ankra generates the Dockerfile, chart and pipeline, builds the image, and deploys it. Pull request previews, branch demos, the application registry, publishing an application as an add-on, builds on Ankra's own builders and the Claude Design import come with it.

[Applications →](/concepts/applications)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-30" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Self-managed AWS clusters are available to every organisation" }}>
## Self-managed AWS clusters are available to every organisation

Ankra-built k3s and kubeadm clusters on EC2 are out of closed beta. Every organisation now sees AWS EC2 next to Amazon EKS in the create-cluster dialog, and the `ankra cluster aws` commands and the AWS cluster endpoints are served for everyone.

[AWS clusters →](/guides/aws-clusters)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-30" tags={["AI"]} rss={{ title: "The AI section is available to every organisation" }}>
## The AI section is available to every organisation

The **AI** section of the sidebar is out of closed beta: Home, Board, Chat, Inbox, Automations, AI Agents and Settings are on for every organisation. That brings the AI board and its agents, automations, AI code review, Slack and Teams connections, ticket sync, and chat autonomy, where low-risk writes can run without a confirmation once you pick **Auto**.

[Ankra AI overview →](/platform/ai-overview)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-30" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Cluster Mesh and multi-zone UpCloud clusters are available to every organisation" }}>
## Cluster Mesh and multi-zone UpCloud clusters are available to every organisation

Cluster Mesh and the WireGuard overlay it runs on are out of closed beta. Any organisation can join its clusters into one mesh, and stretch an UpCloud cluster across a pool of zones. New Ankra-built clusters get a network identity from the organisation's pools, so they can join a mesh later.

[Cluster Mesh →](/platform/cluster-mesh)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-30" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Cloning a stack with its data is available to every organisation" }}>
## Cloning a stack with its data is available to every organisation

Cloning with data is out of closed beta. The clone dialog's **Data** step and `--with-data` carry a stack's volumes and database contents to the target through a backup vault, for every organisation.

[Clone a stack →](/guides/clone-stack)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-30" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Log sources are available to every organisation" }}>
## Log sources are available to every organisation

Log sources are out of closed beta. Any organisation can connect a cluster to Loki, Elasticsearch or OpenSearch under **Settings** → **Integrations**, search them from the **Search** tab on the cluster's **Logs** page, and let Ankra's AI query them. **Log Source** is no longer marked **Coming soon**.

[Log sources →](/integrations/log-sources)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-30" tags={["API", "Security"]} rss={{ title: "The organisation audit log can be exported with an API token, so SIEM forwarding can run on a schedule" }}>
## The organisation audit log can be exported with an API token, so SIEM forwarding can run on a schedule

The audit-log export only accepted a signed-in browser session, so a scheduled job holding a token was redirected to the sign-in page. `GET /api/v1/org/organisation/audit-logs/export` now takes a service token or personal API token holding `audit.read`, with the same filters, CSV and JSON formats and 100,000-row cap as the portal download.

[Forwarding to a SIEM →](/security/audit-export#forwarding-to-a-siem)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-30" tags={["AI", "API"]} rss={{ title: "MCP clients hand you portal links" }}>
## MCP clients hand you portal links

The Ankra MCP server now tells clients to give you a link to the portal page for what they are describing. `list_clusters` and `get_cluster_details` return each cluster's `portal_url`, and the new read-only `get_portal_url` tool returns the address of any other page - a stack, an add-on, a pod, an application, a pipeline run, a ticket, an alert or a CVE - opening in the right organisation. A new Portal URLs page lists every page and how its address is built.

[Portal URLs →](/platform/portal-urls)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-29" tags={["AI", "Pipelines"]} rss={{ title: "Bitbucket Cloud pull requests reach Ankra AI review, mention replies and pipelines" }}>
## Bitbucket Cloud pull requests reach Ankra AI review, mention replies and pipelines

Ankra's Bitbucket webhooks subscribed to pushes only, so reviews, `@ankraai` replies and pull request pipelines never started. Webhooks now carry pull request events and are registered for repositories with AI review on or connected to Ankra Pipelines. Drafts are honoured, replies thread under the question, and `event.provider` reads `bitbucket`, as documented. Reconnect each Bitbucket credential to grant the new pull request permission; until then, GitOps sync keeps working.

[Grant the pull request permission →](/integrations/bitbucket-cloud#granting-the-pull-request-permission)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-29" tags={["GitOps", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "Connecting Bitbucket Cloud works end to end again" }}>
## Connecting Bitbucket Cloud works end to end again

Repository lists froze once Atlassian removed the workspace listing Ankra read, and GitOps settings could not save a Bitbucket repository. Both work again, with a branch picker. Organisation credentials can be deleted, their webhooks removed first; tokens refresh before they expire; a connection must be finished by the Ankra user who started it; and **View source** opens on bitbucket.org.

[Bitbucket Cloud →](/integrations/bitbucket-cloud)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-29" tags={["Clusters", "CLI"]} rss={{ title: "ankra cluster exec runs one command in a pod and exits with its exit code" }}>
## `ankra cluster exec` runs one command in a pod and exits with its exit code

`ankra cluster exec <pod> -n <namespace> -- <command> [args...]` runs one command in a pod - a `psql -c`, a `curl` to localhost, reading a mounted file - and exits with its exit code, so scripts and CI can branch on it. The command reaches the container exactly as given, its output streams as it runs, and every run is audited like a terminal session. It needs CLI 0.20.0 and cluster agent 2.1.1166 or newer.

[One-off commands →](/platform/pod-terminal#one-off-commands)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-29" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Ankra Cloud is a cluster provider, managed and self-managed" }}>
## Ankra Cloud is a cluster provider, managed and self-managed

The create-cluster dialog offers Ankra Cloud two ways. **Ankra Cloud Kubernetes** is a control plane the Ankra team runs, with node pools, autoscaling and upgrades driven from Ankra. **Ankra Cloud** builds kubeadm or k3s on Ankra Cloud servers behind a bastion and a NAT router, with the cloud controller and CSI driver deployed for you. One Ankra Cloud credential serves both. It is in closed beta, enabled per organisation on request.

[Ankra Cloud Clusters →](/guides/ankra-cloud-clusters)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-29" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Domains you hold in Avura can serve your clusters and root their generated domains" }}>
## Domains you hold in Avura can serve your clusters and root their generated domains

Connect an Avura workspace to its Ankra organisation from Avura, then share domains one at a time. A shared domain can be added as a custom DNS zone with no credential of your own, its records are edited under Settings > Avura, and it can be the Custom Ankra domain without delegating it to Ankra's nameservers. Ankra sees only the domains you share, and disconnecting is refused while anything still uses them.

[Use Avura domains with Ankra →](/guides/avura-domains)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-28" tags={["Clusters", "Fixes"]} rss={{ title: "A restore point you take by hand is kept, and a restore stays undoable for a week" }}>
## A restore point you take by hand is kept, and a restore stays undoable for a week

A second **Back up now** in one afternoon used to expire the first within minutes, and with it the safety restore point taken before an in-place restore. Retention now counts only scheduled restore points: one you take yourself is kept for the policy's longest period, and a safety restore point for seven days. The Backups tab also names each restore point and says whether it can still be restored.

[Protect a stack →](/guides/backups#protect-a-stack)
</Update>

<Update label="2026-09-28" tags={["Clusters"]} rss={{ title: "Deleting a cluster names its persistent volumes and deletes them only once you accept it" }}>
## Deleting a cluster names its persistent volumes and deletes them only once you accept it
